is identical to the calculation of
\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u+1]
. This is split into 3 cases, depending on which Frame Constraint is required for the calculation.

Case 1:

\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u-1]\neq 0
.

In this case, rearranging
FC1
implies

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u+1]\penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}FC1\\
=\end{subarray}\penalty\ \penalty\ \frac{\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u]^{2}-\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i-1,u]\cdot\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i+1,u]}{\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u-1]}

\displaystyle=\frac{W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(R,C_{0})}(\textbf{G})[u,i]^{2}-W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(R,C_{0})}(\textbf{G})[u,i-1]\cdot W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(R,C_{0})}(\textbf{G})[u,i+1]}{W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(R,C_{0})}(\textbf{G})[u-1,i]}

\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}FC1\\
=\end{subarray}\penalty\ W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(R,C_{0})}(\textbf{G})[u+1,i]

as required.

Case 2:

\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u-1]=0\neq\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u]
:

In this case, there is a window
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
of size
1\leq l^{\prime}\leq l+2
in the first
u
columns of
\mathfrak{P}_{east}
, and
\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u+1]
is in the eastern outer frame of
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
. That is,
\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u+1]=\widetilde{G}_{\overline{k}}
for some
0\leq\overline{k}\leq l+1
, as illustrated below:

Figure 12.
The window
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
and its inner and outer frames. For now, ignore the orange line.

From the induction hypothesis and equation (
7.6
), there is also a window
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
of equal size in the first
u
rows of
W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(R,C_{0})}(\textbf{G})
and
W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(R,C_{0})}(\textbf{G})[u+1,i]=H^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
for some
0\leq k^{\prime}\leq l+1
.

Figure 13.
The window
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
and its inner frame. For now, ignore the orange line.

Also by the induction hypothesis, the portion of Figure
12
to the left of the orange line is the
90^{\circ}
anticlockwise rotation of the portion of Figure
13
above the orange line, as illustrated below:

Figure 14.
The window
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
with its inner and outer frame relabelled with notation from
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
. Note, the south outer frame of
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
(the sequence
(H_{i}^{\prime})_{0\leq i\leq l+1}
) has not been relabelled.

Therefore,
\overline{k}=l+1-k^{\prime}
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{{\overline{k}}}=C^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{{\overline{k}}}=A_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{{\overline{k}}}=D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}_{{\overline{k}}}=B_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}_{{\overline{k}}}=G_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{{\overline{k}}}=E_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{{\overline{k}}}=F_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}^{-1}=R^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}^{-1}=P^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}^{-1}=S^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}^{-1}=Q^{\prime}

To complete the inductive step in this case, one needs to confirm that
\widetilde{G}_{\overline{k}}=H^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\overline{k}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \begin{subarray}{c}FC3\\
=\end{subarray}\penalty\ \frac{(-1)^{\overline{k}}\frac{\widetilde{Q}\widetilde{E}_{\overline{k}}}{\widetilde{A}_{\overline{k}}}+\frac{\widetilde{P}\widetilde{F}_{\overline{k}}}{\widetilde{B}_{\overline{k}}}-(-1)^{\overline{k}}\frac{\widetilde{R}\widetilde{H}_{\overline{k}}}{\widetilde{D}_{\overline{k}}}}{\frac{\widetilde{S}}{\widetilde{C}_{\overline{k}}}}

(7.7)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \frac{(-1)^{\overline{k}}\frac{(P^{\prime})^{-1}G^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{C^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}+\frac{(R^{\prime})^{-1}E^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{\overline{k}}\frac{(S^{\prime})^{-1}F^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}{\frac{(Q^{\prime})^{-1}}{D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}.

Substituting in
(P^{\prime})^{-1}=(-1)^{l}\cdot\frac{S^{\prime}}{R^{\prime}Q^{\prime}}
from Theorem
4.2
and multiplying both the numerator and denominator by
R^{\prime}Q^{\prime}
gives

(7.8)

\displaystyle(\ref{eqn: rot_lem_2})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{\overline{k}-l}\frac{S^{\prime}G^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{C^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}+\frac{Q^{\prime}E^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{\overline{k}}\frac{R^{\prime}Q^{\prime}F^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{S^{\prime}B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}{\frac{R^{\prime}}{D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}.

One again using
P^{\prime}(-1)^{-l}=\frac{R^{\prime}Q^{\prime}}{S^{\prime}}
from Theorem
4.2
yields

(7.9)

\displaystyle(\ref{eqn: rot_lem_3})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{\overline{k}+l}\frac{S^{\prime}G^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{C^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}+\frac{Q^{\prime}E^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{\overline{k}-l}\frac{P^{\prime}F^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}{\frac{R^{\prime}}{D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}.

Finally, using that
(-1)^{l-\overline{k}}=(-1)^{\overline{k}-l}=(-1)^{\overline{k}+l}
, one obtains

\displaystyle(\ref{eqn: rot_lem_4})

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{Q^{\prime}E^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}+(-1)^{l+1-\overline{k}}\frac{P^{\prime}F^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{l+1-\overline{k}}\frac{S^{\prime}G^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{C^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}{\frac{R^{\prime}}{D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}FC3\\
=\end{subarray}\penalty\ H^{\prime}_{\bar{k}}

as required, since
\overline{k}=l+1-k^{\prime}
.

Case 3:

\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u-1]=0=\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u]
:

This case is very similar to the previous one, with the only difference being that
FC2
is used in place of
FC3
. Once again, there is a window
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
of size
1\leq l^{\prime}\leq l+2
in the first
u
columns of
\mathfrak{P}_{east}
, but this time
\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u+1]
is in the eastern
inner
frame of
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
. That is,
\mathfrak{P}_{east}[i,u+1]=\widetilde{C}_{\overline{k}}
for some
0\leq\overline{k}\leq l+1
. Also as before, the induction hypothesis implies and equation (
7.6
) imply that there is also a window
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
of equal size in the first
u
rows of
W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(R,C_{0})}(\textbf{G})
and
W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(R,C_{0})}(\textbf{G})[u+1,i]=D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
for some
0\leq k^{\prime}\leq l+1
.

Therefore, the goal is to show that
\widetilde{C}_{\overline{k}}=D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
, where
k^{\prime}=l+1-\overline{k}
. This is achieved via the
FC2
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{\overline{k}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \begin{subarray}{c}FC2\\
=\end{subarray}\penalty\ \penalty\ \frac{(-1)^{-l\cdot\overline{k}}\widetilde{A}_{\overline{k}}\cdot\widetilde{D}_{\overline{k}}}{\widetilde{B}_{\overline{k}}}

(7.10)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ =\penalty\ \penalty\ \frac{(-1)^{-l\cdot\overline{k}}C^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\cdot B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}\cdotp

Next, note that by splitting into cases where
l
is odd or even, one obtains that
(-1)^{l\cdot\overline{k}}=(-1)^{l(l+1-\overline{k})}=(-1)^{l\cdot k^{\prime}}
. Hence

\displaystyle(\ref{eqn: rot_lem_5})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{l\cdot k^{\prime}}C^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\cdot B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}FC2\\
=\end{subarray}\penalty\ \penalty\ D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}

as required.

Lemma
7.14
Parts 3 and 4:

The remainder of the proof is omitted, as is it extremely similar to that of
\mathfrak{P}_{east}
: once again, each part is split into three cases depending on which frame constraint is used. The first case is exactly the same method, up to the rearrangement of
FC1
. Both the second and third cases are similar in method, as they imply the existence of two windows
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
and
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
. The only difference is in the relationships between the inner and outer frames of
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
and
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
. In part 3, one now has that

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{k}=D^{\prime}_{k}

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{k}=C_{k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{k}=B^{\prime}_{k}

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}_{k}=A_{k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{k}=G_{k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{k}=F_{l}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{k}=E_{k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}=S^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}=R^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}=Q^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}=P^{\prime}

and the goal is to show that
\widetilde{E}_{k}=H^{\prime}_{k}
. Note that, in this case
\overline{k}=k^{\prime}
and so both notations are replaced by
k
. Similarly for part 4, one has that

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{\overline{k}}=B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{\overline{k}}=D_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{\overline{k}}=A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}_{\overline{k}}=C_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}_{\overline{k}}=F_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\overline{k}}=E_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{\overline{k}}=G_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}^{-1}=Q^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}^{-1}=S^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}^{-1}=S^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}^{-1}=Q^{\prime}

and the goal is to show that
\widetilde{F}_{\overline{k}}=H^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
.
∎

7.4.
Number Walls of One-Sided Sequences

The following notation is introduced to save space on upcoming diagrams.

Notation:
Let
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
and let
\textbf{S}=(s_{i})_{0\leq i\leq n}
be a sequence. Furthermore, let
r,a\in\mathbb{K}
. Then, let

s^{(r,a)}_{i}=a\cdot r^{i}\cdot s_{i}.

As seen earlier in Section
3.4
, one can associate a sequence
\textbf{S}=(s_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
over
\mathbb{K}
to a Laurent series
\Theta(t)=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}s_{i}t^{-i}\in\mathbb{K}((t^{-1}))
. As
\mathbb{K}((t^{-1}))
is a field, there exists a unique
\Xi(t)\in\mathbb{K}((t^{-1}))
such that
\Theta(t)\cdot\Xi(t)=1
. Let
U
be the sequence over
\mathbb{K}
associated to
\Xi(t)
.

Recall the doubly infinite sequence
\textbf{S}^{L}
defined in equation (
3.3
). The goal of this section is to see how the number wall
W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{S}^{L})
relates to
W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{U})
.

7.4.1.
Columns 0 and 1 of
W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(r_{0},a_{0})}\left(\mathbf{S}{(r_{1},a_{1})}\right)

To begin, it is shown that the sequence
U
appears in
W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{S})
.

Lemma 7.16
.

Let
a_{0},r_{0},a_{1},r_{1}\in\mathbb{K}
such that
a_{0},r_{0}\neq 0
and let
\mathbf{S}=(s_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
be a sequence in
\mathbb{K}
such that
s_{0}\neq 0
and
s_{i}=0
for
i<0
. Furthermore, let
\textbf{U}=(u_{i})_{i\geq 0}
be as above. Then,

(7.11)

W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(r_{0},a_{0})}\left(\left(\mathbf{S}{(r_{1},a_{1})}\right)^{L}\right)[m,0]=a_{1}s_{0}\cdot\left(\frac{a_{1}s_{0}}{a_{0}}\right)^{m}

and

(7.12)

c_{m}:=W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(r_{0},a_{0})}\left(\left(\mathbf{S}{(r_{1},a_{1})}\right)^{L}\right)[m,1]=-a_{1}r_{1}s_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{-s_{0}a_{1}r_{1}}{r_{0}a_{0}}\right)^{m}u_{m+1}.

Proof.

The setup is depicted below.

Figure 15.
Each square in the grid is an entry in the number wall. The value of the entries denoted with a blue
X
are easily calculable, but are not ever used in this proof.

Equation (
7.11
) follows immediately from the fact that
\left(W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(r_{0},a_{0})}\left(\left(\mathbf{S}{(r_{1},a_{1})}\right)^{L}\right)[m,0]\right)_{m\geq 0}
is the eastern inner frame of the infinite window
\left(W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(r_{0},a_{0})}\left(\left(\mathbf{S}{(r_{1},a_{1})}\right)^{L}\right)[m,n]\right)_{n<0,m\geq 0}
and is hence a geometric sequence with initial values
W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(r_{0},a_{0})}\left(\left(\mathbf{S}{(r_{1},a_{1})}\right)^{L}\right)[-1,0]=a_{0}
and

W_{\mathbb{K}}^{(r_{0},a_{0})}\left(\left(\mathbf{S}{(r_{1},a_{1})}\right)^{L}\right)[0,0]=a_{1}s_{0}
.

Moving on to equation (
7.12
), one uses the determinant definition of a number wall (Definition
1.5
) and Lemma
7.5
to show that

(7.13)

c_{m}=a_{1}r_{1}\left(\frac{a_{1}r_{1}}{r_{0}a_{0}}\right)^{m}\begin{vmatrix}s_{1}&s_{2}&\dots&\dots&s_{m}&s_{m+1}\\
s_{0}&s_{1}&\ddots&&&s_{m}\\
0&s_{0}&\ddots&\ddots&&\vdots\\
\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\vdots&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&s_{2}\\
0&\dots&\dots&0&s_{0}&s_{1}\end{vmatrix}.

To conclude the proof, the value of the matrix determinant in (
7.13
) is shown to be
(-1)^{m+1}s_{0}^{m+2}u_{m+1}
by induction. This formulae is already known as Wronski’s Formulae
[
Hen86
, Theorem 1.3]
, but, since it is elementary, the proof is repeated here for the sake of completion.

First, it is elementary from the definition of
U
to see that
u_{i}
is generated by the following recurrence relation:

(7.14)

u_{0}=s_{0}^{-1},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ u_{m}=-\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}s_{j}u_{m-j}}{s_{0}}.

The initial conditions of the induction are given by the case
m=0
:

\det(s_{1})=s_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{birecur})}}{{=}}-\frac{s_{0}u_{1}}{u_{0}}=-s_{0}^{2}u_{1}.

For the inductive step, repeatedly take the determinant along the first column. This yields

\displaystyle\begin{vmatrix}s_{1}&s_{2}&\dots&\dots&s_{m}&s_{m+1}\\
s_{0}&s_{1}&\ddots&&&s_{m}\\
0&s_{0}&\ddots&\ddots&&\vdots\\
\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\vdots&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&s_{2}\\
0&\dots&\dots&0&s_{0}&s_{1}\end{vmatrix}

\displaystyle=s_{1}(-1)^{m}s_{0}^{m+1}u_{m}-s_{0}\begin{vmatrix}s_{2}&s_{3}&\dots&\dots&s_{m}&s_{m+1}\\
s_{0}&s_{1}&\dots&\dots&\dots&s_{m-1}\\
0&s_{0}&\ddots&&&\vdots\\
\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\vdots&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&s_{2}\\
0&\dots&\dots&0&s_{0}&s_{1}\end{vmatrix}

\displaystyle=s_{1}(-1)^{m}s_{0}^{m+1}u_{m}-s_{2}s_{0}(-1)^{m-1}s_{0}^{m}u_{m-1}+s_{0}^{2}\begin{vmatrix}s_{3}&s_{4}&s_{5}&\dots&s_{i}\\
s_{0}&s_{1}&s_{2}&\dots&s_{m}\\
0&s_{0}&s_{1}&\dots&s_{m-1}\\
\vdots&&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\dots&s_{0}&s_{1}\end{vmatrix}

(7.15)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m}s_{0}^{m+1}\sum_{j=1}^{m+1}s_{j}u_{m+1-j}.

By the recurrence relation (
7.14
),

(\ref{invlem2})=(-1)^{m+1}s_{0}^{m+2}u_{m+1}.

Hence, equation (
7.13
) becomes

c_{m}=-\left(\frac{-s_{0}a_{1}r_{1}}{r_{0}a_{0}}\right)^{m}a_{1}r_{1}s_{0}^{2}u_{m+1}

as required.
∎

7.4.2.
Lemma
7.16
Applied to Finite Windows

Lemma
7.16
stipulates that the sequence
\mathbf{S}{(r_{1},a_{1})}
appear in row zero of the number wall. By thinking of the rows with index
m<-1
as an infinite window
\mathcal{W}
, the sequence
\mathbf{S}{(r_{1},a_{1})}
can be interpreted as the southern outer frame of
\mathcal{W}
. This motivates the following natural generalisation of Lemma
7.16
, in which
\mathbf{S}{(r_{1},a_{1})}
is a finite sequence appearing in the southern outer frame of a finite window.

Corollary 7.17
.

Let
X
be a sequence over
\mathbb{K}
, and let
l\geq 3
be a natural number. Assume that
W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})
has two windows,
\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{2}
, of size
l-2
and
l
respectively, such that the north east corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
overlaps with the south west corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
, as depicted in Figure
16
.

Next, let
a_{0},r_{0},a_{1},r_{1}\in\mathbb{K}
be such that
a_{0},r_{0}\neq 0
, and let
\mathbf{S}=(s_{i})_{i\geq 0}
be a sequence over a field
\mathbb{K}
with
s_{0}\neq 0
. Additionally, let
m
and
n
be the respective row and column index of the entry of the number wall that is in the inner frame of both
\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{2}
. Assume that

(W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[m,n+i])_{0\leq i\leq l}=(a_{0}r_{0}^{i})_{0\leq i\leq l}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ and }\penalty\ \penalty\ (W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[m+1,n+i])_{0\leq i\leq l}=(a_{1}r_{1}^{i}s_{i})_{0\leq i\leq l}.

Then,

(7.16)

\left(W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[m+i,n]\right)_{0\leq i\leq l-1}=\left(a_{0}\cdot\left(\frac{a_{1}s_{0}}{a_{0}}\right)^{i}\right)_{0\leq i\leq l-1}

and

(7.17)

(d_{i})_{0\leq i\leq l}:=\left(W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[m+i,n+1]\right)_{0\leq i\leq l}=\left(a_{0}r_{0}s_{0}u_{i}\left(\frac{-s_{0}a_{1}r_{1}}{r_{0}a_{0}}\right)^{i}\right)_{0\leq i\leq l},

where
\textbf{U}=(u_{i})_{i\geq 0}
is the unique sequence over
\mathbb{K}
such that
\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}s_{i}t^{-i}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}u_{i}t^{-i}=1
. Finally, one has

(7.18)

\displaystyle W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[m+j,n+i+1]=W^{\left(\frac{a_{1}s_{0}}{a_{0}},a_{1}s_{0}\right)}_{\mathbb{K}}\left(\textbf{U}^{\left(\frac{-s_{0}a_{1}r_{1}}{r_{0}a_{0}},a_{0}r_{0}s_{0}\right)}\right)[i,j]

for
{0\leq i\leq\left\lfloor\frac{l+1}{2}\right\rfloor}
and
i\leq j\leq l-i
.

Figure 16.
A drawing of the setup of Corollary
7.17
. Above,
\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{2}
are the left an right red squares, respectively, and the crossed dots indicate that an unknown entry is known to not be equal to zero.

Proof.

First, note that equation (
7.18
) is achieved by applying Lemma
7.14
with
C
given by equation (
7.16
) and
G
given by equation (
7.17
). With notation from Lemma
7.14
, the portion of the number wall detailed in equation (
7.18
) is
\mathfrak{P}_{east}
.

Furthermore, equation (
7.16
) is immediate as it was in Lemma
7.16
.

Therefore, all that remains is to establish equation (
7.17
). To do this, it is sufficient to show that the value of
d_{i}
is independent of the choice of
m
,
n
and any value denoted by a blue dot in Figure
16
(those being any entry of
(W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[i,j])_{i\leq m-1,j\leq n-1}
,
(W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[m,j])_{j\leq n-1}
or
(W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[i,n])_{i\leq m-1}
). To see why this suffices, note that if this is the case, one can take
m=-1
,
n=0
and pick the values of the blue dots so that Corollary
7.17
follows immediately from Lemma
7.16
(where
d_{i}=c_{i-1}
with
c_{i-1}
being from Lemma
7.16
).

The proof proceeds by induction. In the base case, the calculation of
d_{2}
is independent of
m
,
n
or any entries marked with a blue dot by
FC1
, since
a_{0}r_{0}\neq 0
.

Assume by induction that
d_{2},\dots,d_{i}
are calculated without the use of
m
,
n
and the entries marked by a blue dot. If
d_{i-1}\neq 0
, the the independence of
d_{i+1}
follows from
FC1
.

Hence, assume
d_{i-1}=0
. For now, also assume that
d_{i}\neq 0
. Therefore,
d_{i+1}
is contained in the southern outer frame of a window,
\mathcal{W}_{3}
. Note that
d_{i+1}
is in column
n
and row
m+i+1
. The entry on the same row but column
n-1
is nonzero (as it is in the eastern inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
), and therefore,
d_{i-1}
is the bottom left corner of a window. Applying
FC3
shows that the values of the inner and outer frame that are used in the calculation of
d_{i+1}
do not include the blue dots. This is depicted below.

Figure 17.
The purple square corresponds to
\mathcal{W}_{3}
. It is known that
d_{i-1}
is the bottom right corner of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
. Depicted above is the
maximum
possible size of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
- its actual size is unknown. The entries of the inner and outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
that are used to calculate
d_{i+1}
in this case are highlighted in yellow.

Let
l_{3}
be the side length of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
. With notation from Figure
2
,
d_{i+1}
corresponds to
H_{l_{3}}
in the outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
. Furthermore, the only blue dots that are contained in the inner or outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
are the ones coloured in light blue (
W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[m,n-1]
and
W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[m-1,n]
). These correspond to
E_{0}
and
F_{0}
. By
FC3
, the calculation of
H_{k}
involves
E_{0}
or
F_{0}
if and only if
k=0
, which in this case it is not. Hence,
d_{i+1}
is independent of any blue dot.

When both
d_{i}
and
d_{i-1}
are equal to zero, this either means that
d_{i+1}
is within a window or one must apply
FC2
. In the former case,
d_{i+1}=0
, and in the latter case the proof is identical to the case where
d_{i}\neq 0
but with
FC2
applied instead of
FC3
.

Therefore, the values of
d_{i}
are independent of the entries in the number wall denoted by blue dots, as well as the choices of
m
and
n
, as required.
∎

7.4.3.
Lemma
7.17
Adapted to Infinite Number Walls

By letting
a_{0}=r_{0}=a_{1}=r_{1}=1
and taking
l\to\infty
, the following corollary
8
8

8

This corollary was discovered independently by Erez Nesharim and Viktoria Rudykh at a similar time. A similar result was also shown by Dress
[
Dre65
, Page 5]
in 1965.
of Lemma
7.17
is established.

Corollary 7.18
.

Let
\mathbf{S}=(s_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence over
\mathbb{K}
such that
s_{0}=1
. Furthermore, define the sequence
\textbf{U}=(u_{i})_{\geq 0}
such that

\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}s_{i}t^{-i}\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}u_{i}t^{-i}=1.

Then,

\displaystyle\left(W_{\mathbb{K}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{L}\right)[j,i]\right)_{j\geq-1,i\geq 0}=\left(W_{\mathbb{K}}\left(\textbf{U}^{(-1,1)}\right)[i-1,j+1]\right)_{j\geq-1,i\geq 0}.

Lemma
7.18
is illustrated below.

Figure 18.
Each dot corresponds to an entry of
W_{\mathbb{K}}\left(\mathbf{S}^{L}\right)
. Note, some dots have multiple colours. Each green dot is equal to 1. The orange dots are the sequence
\mathbf{S}
and the black dots are the entries of
W_{\mathbb{K}}\left(\mathbf{S}\right)
. Then, Lemma
7.18
states that the light blue dots are all equal to 1, the dark blue dots are the sequence
(-1)^{i}\cdot u_{i}
and the grey dots the entries of
W_{\mathbb{K}}\left(U^{\left(-1,1\right)}\right)
, reflected horizontally and then rotated clockwise
90^{\circ}
.

8.
Lemmata on the Number Walls of the
p
-Cantor and
p
-Singer Sequences

This section specialises the lemmata from Section
7
to the case where the input sequence is either the
p
-Cantor or the
p
-Singer sequence. This additional specificity allows for stronger results that build upon the work in Section
7
and are crucial for the proof of Theorem
5.2
. From now on, it is necessary to replace the generic field
\mathbb{K}
with the finite field
\mathbb{F}_{p}
. Because of this, the following classical result becomes relevant.

Theorem 8.1

(Fermat’s Little Theorem (FLT
))
.

Let
p
be a prime. Then, for every
h\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
,
x^{p^{h}-1}=1
.

A Note on Presentation

Some of the upcoming lemmata have complex statements. To make each lemma as simple as possible, such results are presented in bullet point format. Additionally, every lemma is illustrated immediately after it is stated. The reader is strongly encouraged to look at the corresponding picture alongside reading the statement of the lemma. Finally, for
h\in\mathbb{N}
, recall that
\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}=(c^{(p)}_{i})_{0\leq i<p^{h}}
and define

(8.1)

\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}=(s^{(p)}_{i})_{0\leq i<p^{h}+2}.

All the sequences (and their variants) that are used in this paper can be found in Appendix
A
.

8.1.
Number Wall of the One-Sided
p
-Cantor Sequence

The first order of business is to establish a version of Lemma
7.17
that is specific to the
p
-Cantor sequence. Both Lemma
7.17
and the upcoming lemma are used in the proof of Theorem
5.2
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let

•

X
be a sequence over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
h\in\mathbb{N}
,

•

a_{0},r_{0},a_{1},r_{1}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
such that
a_{0},r_{0}\neq 0
,

•

\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{2}
be two windows in
W_{p}(\textbf{X})
of size
p^{h}
and
p^{h}-2
respectively, such that

–

\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{2}
are positioned so that the north east corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
overlaps with the south west corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
,

–

m,n\in\mathbb{N}
be the row and column index, respectively, of the entry of
W_{p}(\textbf{X})
which is in the inner frame of both
\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{2}
,

–

the south inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is the
(r_{0},a_{0})
geometric sequence with increasing indices (the sequence goes from
left to right
). That is,

(W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m,n+i])_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}=(a_{0}r_{0}^{i})_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}.

–

the south outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is the
(r_{1},a_{1})
geometric transform of
\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}
with increasing indices (the sequence goes from
left to right
). That is,

(W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+1,n+i])_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}=(a_{1}r_{1}^{i}c^{(p)}_{i})_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}.

Then

•

for every
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
, one has

(8.2)

W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[m+i,n]=a_{0}\cdot\left(\frac{a_{1}}{a_{0}}\right)^{i};

•

for every
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
, one has

(8.3)

d_{i}:=W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i,n+1]=a_{0}r_{0}\left(\frac{-a_{1}r_{1}}{r_{0}a_{0}}\right)^{i}s^{(p)}_{i};

•

for every
{0\leq i\leq\left\lfloor\frac{p^{h}+2}{2}\right\rfloor}
and
i\leq j\leq p^{h}+1-i
, one has

(8.4)

\displaystyle W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+j,n+i+1]=W^{\left(\frac{a_{1}}{a_{0}},a_{0}\right)}_{p}\left(\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}{\left(\frac{-a_{1}r_{1}}{r_{0}a_{0}},a_{0}r_{0}\right)}\right)[i,j].

For the purposes of Figure
19
,
c^{(r_{1},a_{1})}_{i}=c_{i}^{(p)}\cdot r_{1}^{i}\cdot a_{1}
.

Figure 19.
An illustration of Lemma
8.2
. Above, the crossed dots indicate that an unknown entry is known to not be equal to zero. The significance of the orange square is explained later.

Proof.

To begin, equation (
8.2
) follows from the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
being a geometric sequence, and equation (
8.4
) follows from equation (
8.3
) and Lemma
7.14
.

The proof of equation (
8.3
) is very similar to that of equation (
7.12
) in Corollary
7.17
and is therefore mostly omitted, with only the differences being written. The goal is to show that the values of the light-blue, dark-blue or purple dots in Figure
19
are not used in the calculation of
(W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i,n+j])_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1,\penalty\ 0\leq j\leq p^{h}+1-i}
(that is, the green and black dots in Figure
19
). This suffices, as in this case one can pick the light-blue dots to be equal to zero and the purple dots (from left to right) to equal
a_{0}r_{0}^{p^{h}}
,
a_{0}r_{0}^{p^{h}+1}
and
a_{0}r_{0}^{p^{h}+2}
respectively and then the conclusion follows from Corollary
7.17
.

There are two main differences from the proof of Corollary
7.17
. The first comes from Lemma
3.4
and Corollary
3.6
which state that

c^{(p)}_{p^{h}-1}=1\neq 0=c^{(p)}_{p^{h}}=c^{(p)}_{p^{h}+1}.

That is,
c^{(p)}_{p^{h}}
and
c^{(p)}_{p^{h}+1}
are part of a window (call it
\mathcal{W}_{4}
and see the orange square in Figure
19
), implying
(W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i,n+p^{h}-1])_{i=2,3}
are in the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{4}
and hence their value does not depend on the light-blue or purple entries from Figure
19
. The second difference is that the maximum size of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
(recall the proof of Corollary
7.17
) has decreased by 1, since
c_{p^{h}-1}\neq 0
.
∎

8.2.
The
p
-Cantor Sequence between Two Windows

Recall the definition of
\oplus
from equation (
3.2
). The following lemma considers the number wall of a sequence of the form

\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(p)}_{h}(r,a):=\{0\}_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}\oplus\{a\cdot r^{i}\cdot c^{(p)}_{i}\}_{0\leq i<p^{h}}\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}.

Additionally, recall that
p^{(h)}_{2}:=\frac{p^{h}-1}{2}
.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let

•

X
be a sequence over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
of arbitrary length;

•

l,m,n
and
h
be natural numbers,

•

r_{0},a_{0},r_{1},a_{1}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
,

•

\mathcal{W}_{1}
,
\mathcal{W}_{2}
and
\mathcal{W}_{3}
be windows in
W_{p}(\textbf{X})
with side lengths
p^{h}
,
p^{h}-2
and
p^{h}
, respectively, such that

–

\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{3}
have their north west corner on row
m
and that
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is west of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
,

–

the south west corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
overlap with the north east entry of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
, and let this intersection take place on row
m-1
and column
n
,

–

the south east entry in the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
overlaps with the north west entry of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
,

–

the southern inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is given by the
(r_{0},a_{0})
geometric sequence with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is, for
0\leq i<p^{h}
,

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m-1,n+i]=a_{0}r_{0}^{p^{h}-1-i}

–

the southern outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is given by the
(r_{1},a_{1})
geometric transform of
\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}
with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is, for
0\leq i<p^{h}
,

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m,n+i]=a_{1}r_{1}^{p^{h}-1-i}c_{p^{h}-1-i}^{(p)}.

Then,

(1)

The eastern outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
(the sequence
(W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i-1,n+1])_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}
) and the western outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
(the sequence
(W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i-1,n+p^{h}-2])_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}
) are both geometric transforms of
\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}
.

(2)

There is another window in
W_{p}(\textbf{X})
, denoted by
\mathcal{W}_{4}
, of size
p^{h}-2
such that the north west (north east, respectively) corner of its inner frame overlaps with the south east (south west, respectively) corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
(
\mathcal{W}_{3}
, respectively).

(3)

The north outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{4}
is given by the
(r_{1}^{-1},a_{1})
geometric transform of
\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}
with increasing indices (the sequence goes from left to right). That is, for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
,

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+p^{h}-1,n+i]=a_{1}r_{1}^{-i}c_{i}^{(p)}.

(4)

The north inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{4}
is given by the
\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r_{1}^{2}},\frac{a_{1}^{2}}{a_{0}}\right)
geometric sequence with increasing indices (the sequence goes from left to right). That is, for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
,

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+p^{h},n+i]=\frac{a_{1}^{2}r_{0}^{i}}{a_{0}r_{1}^{2i}}.

(5)

when
a_{0},r_{0},a_{1},r_{1}=1
, the region
(W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i,n+j])_{-1\leq i\leq p^{h},\penalty\ 0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1}
has horizontal and vertical symmetry. That is, for all
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
and
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
, one has

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i-1,n+p^{h}-1-j]=W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i-1,n+j]

and

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i-1,n+j]=W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+p^{h}-i,n+j].

Figure 20.
The windows
\mathcal{W}_{1}
,
\mathcal{W}_{2}
and
\mathcal{W}_{3}
are drawn in red, and the green entries are the ones to be calculated. The window
\mathcal{W}_{4}
is also drawn in green. For the sake of not filling the diagram with entry space, the windows are not drawn to scale.

Proof.

As the proof is long, it is split into subsections. To begin, assume that
r_{0},a_{0},r_{1},a_{1}=1
. The full version of Lemma
8.3
is shown to follow from this case later.

The Eastern Outer Frame of

\mathcal{W}_{1}

Note that
c^{(p)}_{p^{h}}=c^{(p)}_{p^{h}+1}=0
for all
h\in\mathbb{N}
(Corollary
3.6
), and hence the conditions of Corollary
8.2
are satisfied with
a_{0},r_{0},a_{1},r_{1}=1
. Therefore, one has

W_{p}(\mathbf{X})[m+i-1,n+1]=(-1)^{i}s^{(p)}_{i}

for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
. That is, the first
p^{h}+1
entries of the eastern outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
(from top to bottom) is the
(-1,1)
-transform of the first
p^{h}-1
entries of the
p
-Singer sequence. This establishes the part
1
of Lemma
8.3
for
\mathcal{W}_{1}
, in the case
r_{0},a_{0},r_{1},a_{1}=1
. Similarly,

W_{\mathbb{K}}(\textbf{X})[m+i-1,n]=1

for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
, as it is the geometric sequence with first two entries given by
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m-1,n]=1
and
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m,n]=c^{(p)}_{0}=1
.

The Western Outer Frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}

Next, from the vertical symmetry of the
p
-Cantor sequence (Lemma
3.4
) and the inherited vertical symmetry of finite number walls (Lemma
7.7
), one has that

W_{p}(\mathbf{X})[m+i,n+j]=W_{p}(\mathbf{X})[m+i,n+p^{h}-1-j]

for all
-1\leq i\leq p^{h}
and
0\leq j\leq p^{h}+1
. That is, the portion of the finite number wall depicted by Figure
20
satisfies vertical symmetry around its midpoint. Hence, one also has that

W_{p}(\mathbf{X})[m+i-1,n+p^{h}-2]=W_{p}(\mathbf{X})[m+i-1,n+1]=(-1)^{i}s^{(p)}_{i}

for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
. This establishes the remainder of part
1
of Lemma
8.3
in the case
r_{0},a_{0},r_{1},a_{1}=1
.

Next, note that since
s^{(p)}_{2i+1}=0
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbf{S}{(-1,1)}(p)=\textbf{S}(p)
. Therefore, the setup is described by the following illustration.

Figure 21.
The same setup as Figure
20
, with the additional information that the east/west outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
/
\mathcal{W}_{3}
is given by the
p
-Singer sequence (blue).

Filing the Space Between Windows

Next, Lemma
7.14
is applied to the east (west, respectively) inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
(
\mathcal{W}_{3}
, respectively) and to the south inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
. This implies that for all
0\leq u\leq p_{2}^{(h)}+1
and for all
u\leq v\leq p^{h}+1-u
, one has

W_{p}(\mathbf{X})[m+v-1,n+u+1]=W_{p}(\mathbf{X})[m+v-1,n+p^{h}-u-2]=W_{p}\left(\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}\right)[u,v],

and similarly, for all
0\leq u\leq p_{2}^{(h)}
,
u\leq v\leq p^{h}-1-u
, one has

W_{p}(\mathbf{X})[m+u,n+v]=W_{p}\left(\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}\right)[u,v].

The portion of the number wall given by
(W_{p}(\mathbf{X})[m+v,n+u])_{-1\leq v\leq p^{h},\penalty\ 0\leq u\leq p^{h}-1}
is depicted below.

Figure 22.
The portion of the number wall given by
(W_{p}(\mathbf{X})[m+v-1,n+u])_{-1\leq v\leq p^{h},\penalty\ 0\leq u\leq p^{h}-1}
. The details of this figure are written below.

Figure
22
is split into 4 sections, denoted by the brown, light-blue, dark-blue and green entries. Some entries are given explicitly, whilst others are denoted by dots. Furthermore, some dots have more then one colour, indicating they are in more than one section. The brown part is given by
W_{p}(\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h})
. By Lemma
7.14
, the light and dark blue parts are given by
W_{p}(\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)})
, rotated
90^{\circ}
in opposite directions. Lemma
8.6
states that the green section is given by
W_{p}(\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h})
. Any of the green section outside the purple boundary is yet to be calculated. The inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
,
\mathcal{W}_{2}
and
\mathcal{W}_{3}
is black and the windows themselves are red. The red and purple lines have additional meanings, which are stated later in the proof.

Filling out Figure
22

The goal is to establish part
5
of Lemma
8.6
: that is, the green section of Figure
22
is a horizontal reflection of the brown section (which is given by
W_{p}\left(\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}\right)
. From this, parts
2
,
3
, and
4
of Lemma
8.6
follow (when
r_{0},a_{0},r_{1},a_{1}=1
). This is done by induction on the rows.

The Base Case:
Lemma
3.4
states that
s^{(p)}_{i}=s^{(p)}_{p^{h}+1-i}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
. Hence, by Lemma
7.7

W_{p}\left(\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}\right)
has vertical symmetry, and therefore, by Lemma
7.14
, the blue sections
9
9

9

Here, “blue sections” refers to both the light and dark blue sections.
of Figure
22
are horizontally symmetric. That is, everything inside the purple border of Figure
22
has horizontal and vertical symmetry. In particular, this implies that the three rows in between the red lines in Figure
22
have horizontal symmetry. That is, row
m+p^{(h)}_{2}+1
is identical to row
m+p^{(h)}_{2}-1
. This serves as the beginning of an induction on the rows.

The Inductive Step:
For some
r\in\mathbb{N}
, assume the rows
m+p^{(h)}_{2}\pm r
rows are horizontally symmetrical. That is, row
m+p^{(h)}_{2}-r+i
is identical to row
m+p^{(h)}_{2}+r-i
for every
0\leq i\leq r
. Consider the rows with index
m+p^{(h)}_{2}\pm(r+1)
. There are two cases: to state them, let
x=W_{p}(S)[m+p^{(h)}_{2}+r+1,n+u]
for some
0\leq u\leq p^{h}-1
be an entry on row
m+p_{2}^{(h)}+r+1
.

Case 1
: The entries of the number wall needed to calculate
x
are contained between rows
m+p^{(h)}_{2}-1
and
m+p^{(h)}_{2}+r
.

Case 2:
Some of the entries of the number wall needed to calculate
x
are found on a row with index less than
m+p^{(h)}_{2}-1
.

Case 1:

Three further sub-cases are needed depending on which Frame Constraint is required to calculate
x
. For the sake of brevity, only the third subcase (corresponding to
FC3
) is completed here, as this is the most complex of the three and the other two are very similar. The setup is illustrated below:

Figure 23.
Row
m+p^{(h)}_{2}
is coloured in green, with rows
m+p^{(h)}_{2}\pm i
in black and grey respectively. Since
FC3
is required,
x
is equal to
H_{k}
in the outer frame of some window
\mathcal{W}
(light red). By the symmetry from the induction hypothesis, there is also a window
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
(dark red) as depicted. The direction of multiplication in the inner frames (recall, these are geometric sequences) are indicated with the black arrows.

Using the notation from Figure
23
, the induction hypothesis about horizontal symmetry implies that

\displaystyle A_{k}=D_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle B_{k}=B_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle C_{k}=C_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle D_{k}=A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle E_{k}=H_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle F_{k}=F_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle G_{k}=G_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle P=(S^{\prime})^{-1}

\displaystyle Q=(Q^{\prime})^{-1}

\displaystyle R=(R^{\prime})^{-1}

\displaystyle S=(P^{\prime})^{-1}

where
k^{\prime}=l+1-k
and
l
is the side length of
\mathcal{W}
. The goal is to show that
H_{k}=E_{k}^{\prime}.
Indeed,

\displaystyle H_{k}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \begin{subarray}{c}\hyperlink{FC3}{FC3}\\
=\end{subarray}\penalty\ \frac{\frac{QE_{k}}{A_{k}}+(-1)^{k}\frac{PF_{k}}{B_{k}}-(-1)^{k}\frac{SG_{k}}{C_{k}}}{R/D_{k}}

(8.5)

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{H^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{Q^{\prime}D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}+(-1)^{k}\frac{F^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{S^{\prime}B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{k}\frac{G^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{P^{\prime}C^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}{\frac{1}{R^{\prime}A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}\cdot

Multiplying the numerator and denominator by
R^{\prime}Q^{\prime}
yields

\displaystyle(\ref{eqn: invwind_case_1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{R^{\prime}H^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}+(-1)^{k}\frac{R^{\prime}Q^{\prime}F^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{S^{\prime}B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{k}\frac{R^{\prime}Q^{\prime}G^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{P^{\prime}C^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}{\frac{Q^{\prime}}{A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}\text{Theorem }\ref{ratio ratio}\\
=\end{subarray}\frac{\frac{R^{\prime}H^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}+(-1)^{k-l}\frac{P^{\prime}F^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{k-l}\frac{S^{\prime}G^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{C^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}{\frac{Q^{\prime}}{A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}\penalty\ \begin{subarray}{c}\hyperlink{FC3}{FC3}\\
=\end{subarray}\penalty\ E_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

where the final equality comes from
k^{\prime}=l+1-k
and
FC3
.

Case 2:

In this case, one has the same picture as in Figure
23
, but now the top row of
\mathcal{W}
has index less than
m+p^{(h)}_{2}
(that is, the top row is above the green dots). By horizontal symmetry, this means the bottom row of
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
has index greater than
m+p^{(h)}_{2}
, and hence
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{W^{\prime}}
intersect. By the square window theorem (Theorem
1.8
),
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
are the same window with top row with index
m+p^{(h)}_{2}+r-1
and bottom row with index
m+p^{(h)}_{2}-r+1
, as illustrated below:

Figure 24.
One window of size
2r+1
, denoted
\mathcal{W}
, has its ‘middle row’ on row
m+p^{(h)}_{2}
(green). Two different sets of inner and outer frame variables are labelled: one in purple and one in pink.

In contrast to Case 1, there are only two sub-cases to be considered, given by which of
FC2
and
FC3
are needed to calculate
x
. For the same reasons as in Case 1, only the sub-case corresponding to
FC3
is completed here. Indeed, in this sub-case
x
is equal to
H_{k}
for the window
\mathcal{W}
. The goal is to show that
H_{k}=E^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
, where

k^{\prime}=l+1-k=2r-k.

Using the notation from Figure
24
, the induction hypothesis implies that

\displaystyle A_{k}=D_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle B_{k}=B_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle C_{k}=C_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle D_{k}=A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle E_{k}=H_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle F_{k}=F_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle G_{k}=G_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle P=S^{-1}

\displaystyle Q^{2}=1

\displaystyle R^{2}=1

The latter two equalities come from the entries of the east/west inner frame of
\mathcal{W}
on rows
m+p_{2}^{(k)}+i
for
i\in\{-1,0,1\}
and the horizontal symmetry from the inductive hypothesis.

Once again, the goal is to show
H_{k}=E_{k}^{\prime}.
Indeed,

\displaystyle H_{k}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \begin{subarray}{c}\hyperlink{FC3}{FC3}\\
=\end{subarray}\penalty\ \frac{\frac{QE_{k}}{A_{k}}+(-1)^{k}\frac{PF_{k}}{B_{k}}-(-1)^{k}\frac{SG_{k}}{C_{k}}}{R/D_{k}}

(8.6)

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{QH^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}+(-1)^{k}\frac{F^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{SB^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{k}\frac{G^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{PC^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}{\frac{R}{A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}\cdot

Multiplying the numerator and denominator by
RQ
and recalling that
R^{2}=Q^{2}=1
yields

\displaystyle(\ref{eqn: invwind_case_3})

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{RH^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}+(-1)^{k}\frac{RQF^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{SB^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{k}\frac{RQG^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{PC^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}{\frac{Q}{A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}\text{Theorem }\ref{ratio ratio}\\
=\end{subarray}\frac{\frac{RH^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{D^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}+(-1)^{k-l}\frac{PF^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{k-l}\frac{SG^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}{C^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}{\frac{Q}{A^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}}=E_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}

as in Case 1.

Other Values of
r_{0},a_{0},r_{1}
and
a_{1}
:

One now establishes parts
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
of Lemma
8.3
in full generality.

The plan is to apply Lemma
7.5
to the statement of Lemma
8.3
when
r_{0},a_{0},r_{1},a_{1}=1
. To do this, note that the proof of Lemma
8.3
so far has been independent of the value of the row index
m
and the column index
n
. Therefore, it is sufficient to establish the claims for any specific choice of
m
and
n
. Indeed, let
m=0
and
n=p^{h}
. In this case, one is considering the
\left(r_{0}^{-1},a_{0}r_{0}^{2p^{h}-1}\right)
-number wall of the
\left(r_{1}^{-1},a_{1}r_{1}^{2p^{h}-1}\right)
-geometric transform of the sequence

\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(p)}_{h}:=\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h}}\oplus\{c^{(p)}_{i}\}_{0\leq i<p^{h}}\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h}},

which itself is denoted
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{h}^{(p)}{\left(r_{0}^{-1},a_{0}r_{0}^{2p^{h}-1}\right)}.
For
0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
one has that

\displaystyle W_{p}^{\left(r_{0}^{-1},a_{0}r_{0}^{2p^{h}-1}\right)}

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{h}^{(p)}{\left(r_{0}^{-1},a_{0}r_{0}^{2p^{h}-1}\right)}\right)\left[p^{h}-1,p^{h}+i\right]

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \begin{subarray}{c}\text{Lemma}\penalty\ \ref{rswall}\\
=\end{subarray}

\displaystyle\frac{r_{1}^{-(p^{h}+i)(p^{h})}(a_{1}r_{1}^{2p^{h}-1})^{p^{h}}}{r_{0}^{-(p^{h}+i)(p^{h}-1)}(a_{0}r_{0}^{2p^{h}-1})^{p^{h}-1}}W_{p}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{h}^{(p)})[p^{h}-1,p^{h}+i]

\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}\hyperlink{FLT}{FLT}\\
=\end{subarray}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle a_{1}r_{1}^{-i}W_{p}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{h}^{(p)})[p^{h}-1,p^{h}+i]

\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}\text{Lemma }\ref{invwind}\\
=\end{subarray}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ a_{1}r_{1}^{-i}c^{(p)}_{i},

and similarly

\displaystyle W_{p}^{\left(r_{0}^{-1},a_{0}r_{0}^{2p^{h}-1}\right)}

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(p)}{\left(r_{1}^{-1},a_{1}r_{1}^{2p^{h}-1}\right)}\right)\left[p^{h},p^{h}+i\right]

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \begin{subarray}{c}\text{Lemma}\ref{rswall}\\
=\end{subarray}

\displaystyle\frac{r_{1}^{-(p^{h}+i)(p^{h}+1)}(a_{1}r_{1}^{2p^{h}-1})^{p^{h}+1}}{r_{0}^{-(p^{h}+i)(p^{h})}(a_{0}r_{0}^{2p^{h}-1})^{p^{h}}}W_{p}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(p)})[p^{h},p^{h}+i]

\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}\hyperlink{FLT}{FLT}\\
=\end{subarray}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{r_{1}^{-2i}a_{1}^{2}}{r_{0}^{-i}a_{0}}W_{p}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(p)})[p^{h}-1+i]

\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}\text{Lemma }\ref{invwind}\\
=\end{subarray}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \frac{r_{0}^{i}a_{1}^{2}}{r_{1}^{2i}a_{0}}

as required.
∎

8.3.
The
p
-Singer Sequence Between two Windows

The following lemma swaps the roles of the
p
-Cantor and
p
-Singer sequences in Lemma
8.3
. As in Lemma
8.3
for the purposes of saving space in diagrams, let
s^{(r,a)}_{i}:=r^{i}\cdot a\cdot s_{i}^{(p)}
.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let

•

X
be a sequence over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
of arbitrary length,

•

l,m,n
and
h
be natural numbers,

•

r_{0},a_{0},r_{1},a_{1}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
,

•

\mathcal{W}_{1}
,
\mathcal{W}_{2}
and
\mathcal{W}_{3}
be windows in
W_{p}(\textbf{X})
with side lengths
p^{h}-2
,
p^{h}
and
p^{h}-2
, respectively, such that

–

\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{3}
have their north west corner on row
m
and that
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is west of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
;

–

the south west corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
overlaps with the north east entry of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
, and that this intersection takes place on row
m-1
and column
n
,

–

the south east entry in the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
overlap with the north west entry of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
,

–

The south inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is given by the
(r_{0},a_{0})
geometric sequence with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is, for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
,

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m-1,n+i]=a_{0}r_{0}^{p^{h}+1-i}.

–

The south outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is given by the
(r_{1},a_{1})
geometric transform of
\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}
with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is, for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
,

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m,n+i]=a_{1}r_{1}^{p^{h}+1-i}s_{p^{h}+1-i}^{(p)}.

Then,

(1)

the eastern outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
(the sequence
(W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i-1,n+1])_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}
) and the western outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
(the sequence
(W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i-1,n+p^{h}])_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}
) are both geometric transforms of
\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}
;

(2)

there is another window in
W_{p}(\textbf{X})
, denoted
\mathcal{W}_{4}
, of size
p^{h}
such that the north west (north east, respectively) corner of its inner frame overlaps with the south east (south west, respectively) corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
(
\mathcal{W}_{3}
, respectively);

(3)

the north outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{4}
is given by the
\left(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{0}^{2}},\frac{a_{0}^{2}r_{0}^{4}}{a_{1}r_{1}^{2}}\right)
geometric transform of
\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}
with increasing indices (the sequence goes from left to right). That is, for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
,

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+p^{h}-3,n+i]=\frac{a_{0}^{2}r_{0}^{4}}{a_{1}r_{1}^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{0}^{2}}\right)^{i}s_{i}^{(p)}.

(4)

the north inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{4}
is given by the
\left(r_{0}^{-1},r_{0}^{2}a_{0}\right)
geometric sequence with increasing indices (the sequence goes from left to right). That is, for for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
,

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+p^{h},n+i]=r_{0}^{2-i}a_{0}.

(5)

when
a_{0},r_{0},a_{1},r_{1}=1
, the region
(W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+i,n+j])_{-1\leq i\leq p^{h}-2,\penalty\ 1\leq j\leq p^{h}}
has horizontal and vertical symmetry;

Lemma
8.4
is illustrated below. Solely for the purposes of this diagram, let
r_{2}:=\frac{r_{1}}{r_{0}^{2}}
and
a_{2}=\frac{a_{0}^{2}r_{0}^{4}}{a_{1}r_{1}^{2}}

Figure 25.
The windows
\mathcal{W}_{1}
,
\mathcal{W}_{2}
and
\mathcal{W}_{3}
are drawn in red. The green entries are the ones to be calculated.

The proof is almost identical to that of Lemma
8.3
and is hence almost entirely omitted.

Proof.

The only difference is that Corollary
7.17
is applied in place of Lemma
8.2
.
∎

8.3.1.
Relating Lemmata
8.3

and

8.4
through rotation.

A simple corollary of Lemmata
8.3
and
8.4
is that
\chi\left(W_{p}\left(\textbf{C}_{h}^{(p)}\right)\right)
and
\chi\left(W_{p}\left(\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}\right)\right)
are related by a rotation. To state this, recall
\rho
from Definition
7.13
.

Corollary 8.5
.

Let
\textbf{X}_{S}
be a sequence over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
such that
W_{p}(\textbf{X}_{S})
has setup identical to that of Lemma
8.4
. Similarly, let
\textbf{X}_{C}
be a sequence over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
such that
W_{p}(\textbf{X}_{C})
has setup identical to that of Lemma
8.3
. Then,

\chi((W_{p}(\textbf{X}_{C})[m+i,n+j])_{0\leq i,j<p^{h}})=\rho(\chi((W_{p}(\textbf{X}_{S})[m+i-1,n+j+1])_{0\leq i,j<p^{h}}))

Proof.

First, recall that Corollary
7.6
shows that for any sequence
S
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and for any
r_{0},a_{0},r_{1},a_{1}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
one has

\chi\left(W^{(r_{0},a_{0})}_{p}\left(\mathbf{S}{(r_{1},a_{1})}\right)\right)=\chi(W_{p}(\textbf{S})).

Therefore, it is sufficient to prove Corollary
8.5
when
r_{0},a_{0},r_{1},a_{1}=1
.

Just as in the proof of Lemma
8.3
,
(W_{p}(\textbf{X}_{C})[m+i,n+j])_{0\leq i,j<p^{h}}
appears as the purple square below

where the interior of this square is filled in by Lemma
7.14
with number walls of
(s_{i})_{1\leq i\leq p^{h}}
and
\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}
as in Figure
22
.

Similarly, Lemma
8.4
states that
(W_{p}(\textbf{X}_{S})[m+i-1,n+j+1])_{0\leq i,j<p^{h}}
appears as the green square below.

Figure 26.
The green square is the
90^{\circ}
rotation of the purple square above.

Since
c_{0}^{(p)}=c_{p^{h}-1}^{(p)}=s_{0}^{(p)}=s_{p^{h}+1}^{(p)}=1
,
c_{1}^{(p)}=c_{p^{h}-2}^{(p)}=s_{1}^{(p)}=s_{p^{h}}^{(p)}=0
, and the
p
-Cantor and
p
-Singer sequences are symmetric (Lemmas
3.4
and
3.10
), one sees that the purple square is the
90^{\circ}
rotation of the green square. Lemma
7.14
fills in both squares and completes the proof.
∎

8.4.
A Window Between Two
p
-Cantor Sequences

The following lemma is equally as important to the proof of Theorem
5.2
as Lemma
8.3
. Lemma
8.6
is illustrated immediately after it is stated and it is strongly recommended to keep this figure in mind whilst reading.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let

•

X
be a sequence of arbitrary length over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
,

•

i\in\{1,2,3\}
, let
h,m,n\in\mathbb{N}
,

•

r_{i},a_{i},r_{L},a_{L},r_{A},a_{A},r_{R},a_{R}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}\backslash\{0\}
,

•

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+1,n+j]=0
, where
j\in\{-p^{h},-p^{h}-1,2p^{h}+1,2p^{h}+2\}
(these are the red zeroes on Figure
27
),

•

\mathcal{W}_{i}
be a window in
W_{p}(\textbf{X})
of size
p^{h}-2
for
i=1,3
and of size
p^{h}
for
i=2
, such that

–

\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{3}
have their bottom left corner on row
m-1
,

–

the south east corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
overlaps with the north west corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
. Let
n
be the column index of this overlap,

–

the south west corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
overlap with the north east corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
,

–

the south inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is the
(r_{L},a_{L})
geometric sequence with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m,n-i]=a_{A}\cdot r_{L}^{i}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
,

–

the south outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is the
(r_{1},a_{1})
-geometric transform of
\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}
with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+1,n-i]=a_{1}\cdot r_{1}^{i}\cdot c_{i}^{(p)}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
,

–

the north inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is the
(r_{A},a_{A})
geometric sequence with increasing indices (the sequence goes from
left to right
). That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m,n+i]=a_{A}\cdot r_{A}^{i}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
,

–

the north outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is the
(r_{2},a_{2})
-geometric transform of
\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}
with increasing indices (the sequence goes from
left to right
). That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m-1,n+i]=a_{2}\cdot r_{2}^{i}\cdot s^{(p)}_{i}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
,

–

the south inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
is the
(r_{R},a_{R})
geometric sequence with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m,n+2p^{h}-i]=a_{R}\cdot r_{R}^{i}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
,

–

the south outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
is the
(r_{3},a_{3})
-geometric transform of
\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}
with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+1,n+2p^{h}-i]=a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{i}\cdot c_{i}^{(p)}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
.

Then

(1)

the southern inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is given by the
\left(\frac{-a_{1}\cdot a_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot a_{A}},\frac{a_{3}^{2}}{a_{R}}\right)
geometric sequence with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is, for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1,

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+p^{h}+1,n+p^{h}+1-i]=\frac{a_{3}^{2}}{a_{R}}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{1}\cdot a_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot a_{A}}\right)^{i}.

(2)

for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
, the
i^{\textup{th}}
term of the southern outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is equal to

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+p^{h}+2,n+p^{h}+1-i]=s^{(p)}_{i}\left(\frac{\frac{a_{1}\cdot a_{2}}{a_{A}^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{A}}\right)^{i}+r_{A}\cdot r_{L}\cdot\left(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{L}}\right)^{i}+\frac{a_{1}\cdot a_{R}\cdot r_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{r_{3}}{r_{R}}\right)^{i}}{\frac{a_{R}^{2}}{a_{3}^{3}}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{1}\cdot a_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot a_{A}}\right)^{-i}}\right)\cdotp

Figure 27.
Each window is depicted with a red square. The inner and outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
are coloured in green and blue, respectively. The southern outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is given by the sequence
(H_{i})_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}
. The green dotted arrows and their labels show the direction of the geometric sequences comprising the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
.

Proof.

Using notation form Figure
2
applied to
\mathcal{W}_{2}
, the goal of parts
1
and
2
is to calculate the sequences
(D_{k})_{0\leq k\leq p^{h}+1}
and
(H_{k})_{0\leq k\leq p^{h}+1}
, respectively. To calculate each entry in the above sequences,
FC2
and
FC3
imply that one needs to know the values of
A_{k},B_{k},C_{k},D_{k},E_{k},F_{k},G_{k},P,Q,R
and
S
. Each of these are either known by assumption or are calculated from the previous lemmas in this section. From the statement of the lemma, the following variables are known:

\displaystyle P=r_{A}

\displaystyle A_{k}=a_{A}\cdot r_{A}^{k}

\displaystyle E_{k}=s^{(r_{2},a_{2})}_{k}=s^{(p)}_{k}\cdot a_{2}\cdot r_{2}^{k}.

Furthermore, for some specific values of
k
, the values of
B_{k}
and
C_{k}
are known.

\displaystyle B_{0}=A_{0}=a_{A}

\displaystyle B_{1}=C^{(\mathbf{r}_{1})}_{0}=a_{1}

\displaystyle C_{p^{h}+1}=a_{R}\cdot r_{R}^{p^{h}-1}=a_{R}

\displaystyle C_{p^{h}}=C^{(\mathbf{r}_{3})}_{p^{h}-1}=a_{3}r_{3}^{p^{h}-1}=a_{3}.

Using these, one obtains that

\displaystyle Q=B_{1}\cdot B_{0}^{-1}=a_{1}\cdot a_{A}^{-1}

\displaystyle R=C_{p^{h}+1}\cdot C_{p^{h}}^{-1}=a_{R}\cdot a_{3}^{-1}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle B_{k}=B_{0}\cdot Q^{k}=a_{1}^{k}\cdot a_{A}^{1-k}

(8.7)

\displaystyle C_{k}=C_{p^{h}+1}\cdot R^{k-p^{h}-1}=a_{R}^{p^{h}-1}\cdot(a_{3}\cdot a_{R}^{-1})^{k-p^{h}-1}=a_{3}^{2-k}\cdot a_{R}^{k-1}.

The next variable to be calculated is
F_{k}
. By applying Lemma
7.7
(vertical reflection of number walls), one is now able to apply Lemma
8.2
. To this end, “
\leftrightarrow
" notation is introduced to make it clear which of the variables from Lemma
8.2
correspond to each variable in Lemma
8.6
: the left side of each
\leftrightarrow
is notation from Lemma
8.2
and the right-side is notation from this proof.

\displaystyle a_{0}\leftrightarrow a_{A}

\displaystyle r_{0}\leftrightarrow r_{L}

\displaystyle a_{1}\leftrightarrow c_{0}^{(r_{1},a_{1})}=a_{1}

\displaystyle r_{1}\leftrightarrow r_{1}

\displaystyle s_{0}\leftrightarrow c^{(p)}_{0}=1

\displaystyle b_{k}\leftrightarrow s^{(p)}_{k}.

Therefore, by Lemma
8.2
,

\displaystyle F_{k}=a_{A}\cdot r_{L}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{1}\cdot r_{1}}{a_{A}\cdot r_{L}}\right)^{k}\cdot s^{(p)}_{k}.

Similarly, one uses Lemma
8.2
(without needing to apply Lemma
7.7
first) to calculate
G_{k}
. Here,

\displaystyle a_{0}\leftrightarrow A_{p^{h}+1}=a_{R}\cdot r_{R}^{p^{h}-1}\begin{subarray}{c}\hyperlink{FLT}{FLT}\\
=\end{subarray}a_{R}

\displaystyle r_{0}\leftrightarrow r_{R}^{-1}

\displaystyle a_{1}\leftrightarrow c^{(r_{3},a_{3})}_{p^{h}-1}=a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{p^{h}-1}\begin{subarray}{c}\hyperlink{FLT}{FLT}\\
=\end{subarray}a_{3}

\displaystyle r_{1}\leftrightarrow r_{3}^{-1}

\displaystyle s_{0}\leftrightarrow c^{(p)}_{p^{h}-1}=1

\displaystyle b_{k}\leftrightarrow s^{(p)}_{k}.

However, note that the index
k
in
G_{k}
increases as the row index decreases. Therefore, one has that

\displaystyle G_{k}

\displaystyle=a_{R}\cdot r_{R}^{-1}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{-1}}{r_{R}^{-1}\cdot a_{R}}\right)^{p^{h}+1-k}s^{(p)}_{p^{h}+1-k}

(8.8)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ Lemma \ref{sym}}}}{{=}}(a_{R}\cdot r_{R}^{-1})^{-1}\cdot(-a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{-1})^{2}\left(\frac{r_{R}^{-1}\cdot a_{R}}{-a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{-1}}\right)^{k}s^{(p)}_{k}.

Next, the values of
D_{k}
and
S
are computed. Using Theorem
4.2
, one has

S=\frac{QR}{P}\cdot(-1)^{p^{h}}=\frac{-a_{1}\cdot a_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot a_{A}}\cdotp

Therefore, using that
D_{0}=C_{0}=C_{p^{h}+1}\cdot R^{-p^{h}-1}
, one obtains that

\displaystyle D_{k}

\displaystyle=D_{0}\cdot S^{k}=a_{R}\cdot\left(\frac{a_{R}}{a_{3}}\right)^{-p^{h}-1}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{1}\cdot a_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot a_{A}}\right)^{k}

(8.9)

\displaystyle=\frac{a_{3}^{2}}{a_{R}}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{1}\cdot a_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot a_{A}}\right)^{k}

This establishes claim
1
of Lemma
8.6
. For claim
2
, recall
FC3
which states

\displaystyle H_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{Q\cdot E_{k}}{A_{k}}+(-1)^{k}\cdot\frac{P\cdot F_{k}}{B_{k}}-(-1)^{k}\cdot\frac{S\cdot G_{k}}{C_{k}}}{\frac{R}{D_{k}}}

(8.10)

\displaystyle=s^{(p)}_{k}\left(\frac{\frac{a_{1}\cdot a_{2}}{a_{A}^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{A}}\right)^{k}+r_{A}\cdot r_{L}\cdot\left(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{L}}\right)^{k}+\frac{a_{1}\cdot a_{R}\cdot r_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{r_{3}}{r_{R}}\right)^{k}}{\frac{a_{R}^{2}}{a_{3}^{3}}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{1}\cdot a_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot a_{A}}\right)^{-k}}\right)

∎

With an additional assumption, Lemma
8.6
is simplified.

Corollary 8.7
.

Consider the same setup as in Lemma
8.6
, but with the additional condition that there exists an
x\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{r_{1}}{r_{L}}=\frac{r_{2}}{r_{A}}=\frac{r_{3}}{r_{R}}=x.

Then, the southern outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
(that is, the sequence
(H_{i})_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}
from Figure
27
) is a geometric transform of
(s^{(p)}_{i})_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}
. In particular,

H_{k}=s_{k}^{(p)}\cdot\left(\frac{-x\cdot r_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot a_{A}}{a_{1}\cdot a_{R}}\right)^{k}\cdot\frac{a_{3}^{3}}{a_{R}^{2}}\cdot\left(1+\frac{a_{1}\cdot a_{R}\cdot r_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{2}}\right).

Proof.

With the additional assumption of
r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{L},r_{A}
and
r_{R}
, one factorises equation (
8.10
) to obtain

(8.11)

\displaystyle H_{k}=s_{k}^{(p)}\cdot\left(\frac{-x\cdot r_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot a_{A}}{a_{1}\cdot a_{R}}\right)^{k}\cdot\underbrace{\left(\frac{\frac{a_{1}\cdot a_{2}}{a_{A}^{2}}+r_{A}\cdot r_{L}+\frac{a_{1}\cdot a_{R}\cdot r_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{2}}}{\frac{a_{R}^{2}}{a_{3}^{3}}}\right)}_{(*)}

When
k=0
, equation (
8.11
) is equal to
H_{0}
, which implies that
(*)=H_{0}
. Using
FC1
to calulcate
H_{0}
directly, one arrives at

(8.12)

\displaystyle H_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{D_{0}^{2}\cdot D_{1}G_{0}}{C_{1}}\cdotp

The proof is completed by applying equations, (
8.8
), (
8.7
) and (
8.9
) to equation (
8.12
).
∎

8.5.
A Window Between Two
p
-Singer Sequences

The following Lemma is similar to Lemma
8.6
, but the roles of the
p
-Cantor and
p
-Singer sequence have been swapped.

Lemma 8.8
.

Let

•

X
be a sequence of arbitrary length over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
;

•

i\in\{1,2,3\}
, let
h,m,n\in\mathbb{N}
;

•

r_{i},a_{i},r_{L},a_{L},r_{A},a_{A},r_{R},a_{R}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}\backslash\{0\}
,

•

\mathcal{W}_{i}
be a window in
W_{p}(\textbf{X})
of size
p^{h}
for
i=1,3
and of size
p^{h}-2
for
i=2
, such that

–

\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{3}
have their bottom left corner on row
m-1
;

–

the south east corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
overlaps with the north west corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
. Let
n
be the column index of this overlap;

–

the south west corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
overlaps with the north east corner of the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
;

–

the south inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is given by the
(r_{L},a_{L})
geometric sequence with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m,n-i]=a_{A}\cdot r_{L}^{i}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
;

–

the south outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is the
(r_{1},a_{1})
-geometric transform of
\textbf{S}^{(p)}_{h}
with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+1,n-i]=a_{1}\cdot r_{1}^{i}\cdot s_{i}^{(p)}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
;

–

the north inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is given by the
(r_{A},a_{A})
geometric sequence with increasing indices (the sequence goes from
left to right
). That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m,n+i]=a_{A}\cdot r_{A}^{i}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
;

–

the north outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is the
(r_{2},a_{2})
-geometric transform of
\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}
with increasing indices (the sequence goes from
left to right
). That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m-1,n+i]=a_{2}\cdot r_{2}^{i}\cdot c^{(p)}_{i}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
;

–

the south inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
is given by the
(r_{R},a_{R})
geometric sequence with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
).. That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m,n+2p^{h}-i]=a_{R}\cdot r_{R}^{i}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
;

–

the south outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{3}
is the
(r_{3},a_{3})
-geometric transform of
\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}
with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is,
W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+1,n+2p^{h}-i]=a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{i}\cdot s_{i}^{(p)}
for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1
;

Then,

•

the southern inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is the
\left(\frac{-a_{1}\cdot a_{R}\cdot r_{R}^{2}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot a_{A}\cdot r_{3}^{2}},a_{R}r_{R}^{2}\right)
geometric sequence with decreasing indices (the sequence goes from
right to left
). That is, for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
,

W_{p}(\textbf{X})[m+p^{h}+1,n+p^{h}+1-i]=a_{R}r_{R}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{1}\cdot a_{R}\cdot r_{R}^{2}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{3}\cdot a_{A}\cdot r_{3}^{2}}\right)^{i},

•

for
0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
, the
i^{\textup{th}}
term of the south outer-frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is equal to

W_{p}[m+p^{h},n+p^{h}-1-i]=c^{(p)}_{k}\left(\frac{\frac{a_{1}\cdot a_{2}}{a_{A}^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{A}}\right)^{k}+r_{A}\cdot r_{L}\cdot\left(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{L}}\right)^{k}+\frac{a_{1}\cdot a_{R}\cdot r_{R}}{a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{2}\cdot r_{A}\cdot a_{A}}\cdot\left(\frac{r_{3}}{r_{R}}\right)^{k}}{\frac{1}{r_{3}^{2}\cdot a_{3}}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{1}\cdot a_{R}\cdot r_{R}^{2}}{a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{2}\cdot r_{A}\cdot a_{A}}\right)^{-k}}\right)\cdotp

Figure 28.
Each window is depicted with a red square. The inner and outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
are coloured in green and blue, respectively. The southern outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is given by the sequence
(H_{i})_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}
. The green dotted arrows and their labels show the direction of the geometric sequences comprising the inner frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
.

Proof.

The proof of Lemma
8.8
is identical to that of Lemma
8.6
, with the exception of Lemma
8.2
being replaced with Lemma
7.17
. Hence, the values of the entries in the inner and outer frames are stated without proof.

\displaystyle A_{k}=a_{A}r_{A}^{k}

\displaystyle E_{k}=a_{2}r_{2}^{k}c_{k}^{(p)}

\displaystyle P=r_{A}

\displaystyle B_{k}=a_{A}\cdot\left(\frac{a_{1}}{a_{A}}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle F_{k}=a_{A}r_{L}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{1}r_{1}}{r_{L}a_{A}}\right)^{k}c_{k}^{(p)}

\displaystyle Q=\frac{a_{1}}{a_{A}}

\displaystyle C_{k}=a_{R}r_{R}^{2}\left(\frac{a_{R}r_{R}^{2}}{a_{3}r_{3}^{2}}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle G_{k}=a_{R}r_{R}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{R}r_{R}}{a_{3}r_{3}}\right)\cdot c_{k}^{(p)}

\displaystyle R=\frac{a_{R}r_{R}^{2}}{a_{3}r_{3}^{2}}

\displaystyle D_{k}=a_{R}r_{R}^{2}\left(\frac{a_{R}a_{1}r_{R}^{2}}{a_{3}a_{A}r_{3}^{2}r_{A}}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle S=-\frac{a_{R}a_{1}r_{R}^{2}}{a_{3}r_{3}^{2}a_{A}r_{A}}\cdotp

∎

Just as with Corollary
8.7
, one can simplify Lemma
8.8
.

Corollary 8.9
.

Consider the same setup as in Lemma
8.8
, but with the additional condition that there exists an
x\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{r_{1}}{r_{L}}=\frac{r_{2}}{r_{A}}=\frac{r_{3}}{r_{R}}=x.

Then, the southern outer frame of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
(that is, the sequence
(H_{i})_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}
from Figure
28
) is a geometric transform of
(c^{(p)}_{i})_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}
. In particular,

H_{k}=c_{k}^{(p)}\cdot\left(\frac{-x\cdot a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{2}\cdot r_{A}\cdot a_{A}}{a_{1}\cdot a_{R}\cdot r_{R}^{2}}\right)^{k}\cdot\left(a_{3}\cdot r_{3}^{2}+\frac{a_{1}\cdot a_{R}\cdot r_{R}}{r_{A}\cdot a_{A}}\right).

9.
The Construction Lemmata

The goal of this section is to provide two lemmata that combine to give the proof of Theorem
5.2
. These are known as the Construction Lemmata, and they build upon the results from the previous section. Before they are stated, some additional notation is defined.

Notation:
For
h\in\mathbb{N}
and
r,a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, let

\displaystyle\textbf{G}_{h,s}(r,a)=\left(a\cdot r^{i}\right)_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}

\displaystyle\textbf{G}_{h,\ell}(r,a)=\left(a\cdot r^{i}\right)_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}

Above, the
s
in
\textbf{G}_{h,s}(r,a)
and the
\ell
in
\textbf{G}_{h,l}(r,a)
stand for “short” and “long”, respectively.

9.1.
Construction Lemma 1

Recall that
p_{2}:=\frac{p-1}{2}
. Construction Lemma 1 combines Lemma
8.3
and Lemma
8.6
. It is illustrated immediately after it is stated, and it is
strongly
advised that the reader keep this figure in mind when reading the statement of Lemma
9.1
.

Lemma 9.1

(Construction Lemma 1
)
.

Let

•

h\geq 1
and
m\geq 0
be natural numbers,

•

S
be a sequence over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
of length
p^{h+1}
,

•

\widetilde{\textbf{S}}
be the sequence of length
3p^{h+1}
defined by
\widetilde{\textbf{S}}:=\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h+1}}\oplus\textbf{S}\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h+1}}
,

•

r_{S,i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
and
a_{S,i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
for
0\leq i<p_{2}
,

•

r_{C,i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
and
a_{C,i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
for
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

•

a_{0}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
, and define

(9.1)

a_{i}:=\prod^{i-1}_{k=0}r_{S,k}^{2}.

Assume that rows
m-2
,
m-1
and
m
of
W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})
have the following structure:

•

Row
m-2
:

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-2,p^{h+1}]\neq 0
, and
W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-2,2p^{h+1}-1]\neq 0
,

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-2,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]_{1\leq j\leq p^{h}-2}=0
for
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-2,p^{h+1}+(2i+1)p^{h}-1+j]=a_{S,i}r_{S,i}^{j}s^{(p)}_{j}
for
{0\leq j\leq p^{h}+1}
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
.

•

Row
m-1
:

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-1,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]=a_{i}r_{C,i}^{p^{h}-1-j}
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-1,p^{h+1}+(2i+1)p^{h}-1+j]=a_{i}r_{S,i}^{j}
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}+1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
.

•

Row
m
:

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]=a_{C,i}\cdot r_{C,i}^{p^{h}-1-j}\cdot c_{j}^{(p)}
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m,(2i+1)p^{h}+j]=0
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
.

Then, rows
m+p^{h}-1
,
m+p^{h}
and
m+p^{h}+1
have the following structure:

(1)

Row
m+p^{h}-1
:

(a)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m+p^{h}-1,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]=a_{C,i}\cdot r_{C,i}^{-j}\cdot c_{j}^{(p)}
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

(b)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m+p^{h}-1,p^{h+1}+(2i+1)p^{h}+j]=0
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
.

(2)

Row
m+p^{h}
:

(a)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m+p^{h},p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]=\frac{a_{C,i}^{2}}{a_{i}}\cdot\left(r_{C,i}\right)^{-j}
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

(b)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-1,p^{h+1}+(2i+1)p^{h}-1+j]=\frac{a_{C,i+1}^{2}}{a_{i+1}}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{C,i}\cdot a_{i+1}}{r_{S,i}\cdot a_{C,i+1}\cdot a_{i}}\right)^{p^{h}+1-j}
for
{0\leq j\leq p^{h}+1}
.

(3)

Row
m+p^{h}+1
:

(a)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-2,p^{h+1}]\neq 0
and
W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-2,2p^{h+1}-1]\neq 0
,

(b)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-2,p^{h+1}+(2i+1)p^{h}-1+j]=s^{(p)}_{j}\cdot x_{i}\cdot\left(\frac{-a_{C,i}\cdot a_{i+1}}{r_{C,i+1}\cdot a_{C,i+1}\cdot a_{i}}\right)^{p^{n}+1-j}
, where
0\leq j\leq p^{h+1}
,
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
and

x_{i}=\frac{a_{C,i+1}^{3}}{a_{i+1}^{2}}\cdot\left(1+\frac{a_{C,i}\cdot a_{i+1}}{r_{S,i}\cdot a_{i}\cdot a_{C,i+1}\cdot r_{C,i+1}}\right);

(c)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m+p^{h}+1,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]_{1\leq j\leq p^{h}-2}=0
for
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
.

Figure 29.
The setup of Lemma
9.1
, with the details described below.

Each dot in the above figure is an entry in
W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})
, with the colour representing which sequence that entry belongs to. Some dots have multiple colours. Any geometric transform of the
p
-Singer (
p
-Cantor, respectively) sequence is in blue (orange, respectively). Geometric sequences are in green, where light (dark, respectively) green indicates a short (long, respectively) sequence. The two sided arrows denote the length of a sequence, whereas the one sided ‘braced’ arrows denote the direction of indexing for the geometric sequence/transform.

Remark 9.2
.

It is worth verifying that equation (
9.2
) is consistent with Figure
29
. Indeed, since the geometric sequences on row
m-1
overlap, one can observe that for
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

a_{i}=a_{i-1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{p^{h}+1}r_{S,i-1}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{p^{h}-1}r_{C,i}^{-1}\right).

Two applications of
FLT
and a simple induction on
i
establishes equation (
9.2
).

Proof.

The proof is split into parts.

Splitting row
m

First, row
m
is split into overlapping parts so that each part matches the setup of either Lemma
8.3
or Corollary
8.7
. To this end, two extra variations of the
p
-Cantor sequence are defined. Let
a,r,a^{\prime},r^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime},r^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}\backslash\{0\}
.

•

\widetilde{\textbf{C}}^{(p)}_{h}(r,a):=\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h}}\oplus\textbf{C}_{h}^{(p)}{(r,a)}\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h}}
;

•

\overline{\textbf{C}}^{(p)}_{h}(r^{\prime},a^{\prime},r^{\prime\prime},a^{\prime\prime}):=\{0\}_{i=1,2}\oplus\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}(r^{\prime},a^{\prime})\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h}}\oplus\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}(r^{\prime\prime},a^{\prime\prime})\oplus\{0\}_{i=1,2}

Row
m
of
W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})
is split into
p
overlapping sections as follows:

Figure 30.

is split into overlapping copies of

and
\overline{\textbf{C}}_{n}^{(p)}(r_{C,i},a_{C,i},r_{C,i+1},a_{C,i+1})
.

Applying Lemma
8.3

to

\widetilde{C}_{h}^{(p)}(r_{C,i},a_{C,i})

For
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
, consider
(W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j])_{-p^{h}\leq j\leq 2p^{h}-1}
(which is equal to
\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{h}^{(p)}(r_{C,i},a_{C,i})
) and
(W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-1,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j])_{0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1}
(which is equal to
\textbf{G}_{h,s}(r_{L,i},a_{L,i})
). This portion of the number wall satisfies the conditions of Lemma

with the following values, where the notation on the left of the “

, and the notation on the right of the “

:

\displaystyle a_{0}\leftrightarrow a_{i}

\displaystyle r_{0}\leftrightarrow r_{C,i}

\displaystyle a_{1}\leftrightarrow a_{C,i}

\displaystyle r_{1}\leftrightarrow r_{C,i}

\displaystyle m\leftrightarrow m.

Therefore, one has the following values on rows
m+p^{h}-1
and
m+p^{h}
:

(1)

Row
m+p^{h}-1
:

(1a)

By Lemma

(

),

and

;

(1b)

,

for

and

.

(2)

Row

(

),

for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

(3)

Row

:

(3c)

By Lemma

),

This is illustrated below:

Applying Lemma

and Corollary

to

\overline{\textbf{C}}^{(p)}_{h}(r_{C,i},a_{C,i},r_{C,i+1},a_{C,i+1})

Much like how Lemma

,
