and completes the proof.
∎

9.2.
Construction Lemma 2

This lemma is similar to Construction Lemma 1 (Lemma
9.1
), but where the roles of the
p
-Cantor and
p
-Singer sequences are reversed.

Lemma 9.3

(Construction Lemma 2
)
.

Let

•

h\geq 1
be a natural number,

•

S
be a sequence over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
of length
p^{h}
,

•

\widetilde{\textbf{S}}
be the sequence of length
3p^{h+1}
defined as

\widetilde{\textbf{S}}:=\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h+1}}\oplus\textbf{S}\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h+1}},

•

r_{S,i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
and
a_{S,i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
for
0\leq i<p_{2}
,

•

r_{C,i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
and
a_{C,i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
for
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

•

a_{0}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
and define

(9.2)

a_{i}:=\prod^{i-1}_{k=0}r_{S,k}^{-2}.

Assume that rows
m-2
,
m-1
and
m
of
W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})
have the following structure:

•

Row
m-2
:

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-2,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]=a_{C,i}\cdot r_{C,i}^{j}\cdot c_{j}^{(p)}
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
;

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-2,p^{h+1}+(2i+1)p^{h}+j]=0
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
.

•

Row
m-1
:

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-1,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]=a_{i}r_{C,i}^{j}
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-1,p^{h+1}+(2i+1)p^{h}-1+j]=a_{i+1}r_{S,i}^{p^{h}-1-j}
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}+1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
.

•

Row
m
:

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m,p^{h+1}]\neq 0
and
W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-2,2p^{h+1}-1]\neq 0
,

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]
for
{1\leq j\leq p^{h}-2}=0
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

–

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m,p^{h+1}+(2i+1)p^{h}-1+j]=a_{S,i}r_{S,i}^{p^{h}+1-j}s^{(p)}_{j}
for
{0\leq j\leq p^{h}+1}
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
.

Then, rows
m+p^{h}-3
,
m+p^{h}-2
and
m+p^{h}-1
have the following structure:

(1)

Row
m+p^{h}-3
:

(a)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m+p^{h}-3,p^{h+1}]\neq 0
and
W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-2,2p^{h+1}-1]\neq 0
,

(b)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m+p^{h}-3,p^{h+1}+(2i+1)p^{h}-1+j]=s^{(p)}_{j}\cdot\frac{a_{i+1}^{2}\cdot r_{S,i}^{2}}{a_{S,i}}\cdot r_{S,i}^{-j}
, with
0\leq j\leq p^{h}+1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
,

(c)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m+p^{n}-3,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]=0
for
{1\leq j\leq p^{h}-2}
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
.

(2)

Row
m+p^{h}-2
:

(a)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m+p^{h},p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]=\left(-\frac{a_{S,i-1}\cdot a_{i+1}}{a_{i}\cdot a_{S,i}\cdot r_{C,i}}\right)^{p^{h}-1-j}\cdot a_{i+1}\cdot r_{S,i}^{2}
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,

(b)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m-1,p^{h+1}+(2i+1)p^{h}-1+j]=r_{S,i}^{2}\cdot a_{i+1}\cdot r_{S,i}^{-j}
for
{0\leq j\leq p^{h}+1}.

(3)

Row
m+p^{h}-1
:

(a)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m+p^{h}-1,p^{h+1}+(2i+1)p^{h}+j]=0
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
,

(b)

W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})[m+p^{h}-1,p^{h+1}+2ip^{h}+j]=y_{i}\cdot\left(-\frac{a_{S,i-1}\cdot a_{i+1}}{a_{i}\cdot a_{S,i}\cdot r_{C,i}}\right)^{p^{h}-1-j}\cdot c^{(p)}_{p^{h}-1-j}
for
0\leq j\leq p^{h}-1
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
, where

y_{i}=r_{S,i}^{2}\cdot a_{S,i}\left(1+\frac{a_{S,i-1}\cdot a_{i+1}}{a_{S,i}\cdot r_{S,i}\cdot r_{C,i}\cdot a_{i}}\right).

Figure 32.
The setup of Lemma
9.1
. Each dot is an entry in
W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{S}})
, with the colour representing which sequence that entry belongs to. Some dots have multiple colours. Any geometric transform of the
p
-Singer (
p
-Cantor, respectively) sequence is in blue (orange, respectively). Geometric sequences are in green, with the shade being used to distinguish one geometric sequence from another. The two sided arrows denote the length of a sequence, whereas the one sided arrows denote the direction of indexing for the geometric sequence/transform.

Proof.

The proof is identical to that of Construction Lemma
1
, with Lemma
8.4
, Lemma
8.8
and Corollary
8.9
used in place of Lemma
8.3
, Lemma
8.6
and Corollary
8.7
respectively.
∎

10.
Proof of Theorem
5.2

Recall that
\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{h}^{(p)}:=\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h}}\oplus\textbf{C}_{h}^{(p)}\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h}}
. Theorem
5.2
is established by showing that for every
h\geq 0
, one has

(10.1)

\chi\left(\left(W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{h}^{(p)}\right)[m,n]\right)_{0\leq m<p^{h},\penalty\ p^{h}\leq n<2p^{h}}\right)=\Pi(\Phi^{h}(A)).

This is done by induction on
h
.

10.1.
The Base Case

When
h=0
, equation (
10.1
) is trivial. In general, the
h=i
case follows from applying Construction Lemmata
1
and
2
to the
h=i-1
case. However, this does not hold in the
h=1
case, since this would involve windows of size
-2
. Therefore, the
h=1
case is established independently and serves as the base case for the induction. This is achieved by explicitly calculating the value of every entry in
\left(W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{1}^{(p)}\right)[m,n]\right)_{-1\leq m<p,\penalty\ p\leq n<2p}
.

To this end, each entry in
W_{p}\left(\mathcal{C}_{1}\right)
is categorised into one of four families of variables, depending on the parity of its row and column index modulo 2. In particular, let

\displaystyle a_{i,j}:=W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{1}^{(p)}\right)[2j-1,p+2i]\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ for }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq i\leq p_{2}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ and }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq j\leq p_{2},

\displaystyle b_{i,j}:=W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{1}^{(p)}\right)[2j-1,p+2i+1]\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ for }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ and }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq j\leq p_{2},

\displaystyle c_{i,j}:=W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{1}^{(p)}\right)[2j,p+2i]\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ for }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq i\leq p_{2}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ and }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq j\leq p_{2},

\displaystyle d_{i,j}:=W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{1}^{(p)}\right)[2j,p+2i+1]\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ for }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ and }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq j\leq p_{2}.

That is,
\left(W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{1}{(p)}\right)[m,n]\right)_{-1\leq m<p,\penalty\ p\leq n<2p}
appears as follows:

\begin{matrix}\text{Row }-1&a_{0,0}&b_{0,0}&a_{1,0}&b_{1,0}&\cdots&a_{p_{2}-1,0}&b_{p_{2}-1,0}&a_{p_{2},0}\\
\text{Row }0&c_{0,0}&d_{0,0}&c_{1,0}&d_{1,0}&\cdots&c_{p_{2}-1,0}&d_{p_{2}-1,0}&c_{p_{2},0}\\
&a_{0,1}&b_{0,1}&a_{1,1}&b_{1,1}&\cdots&a_{p_{2}-1,1}&b_{p_{2}-1,1}&a_{p_{2},1}\\
&c_{0,1}&d_{0,1}&c_{1,1}&d_{1,1}&\cdots&c_{p_{2}-1,1}&d_{p_{2}-1,1}&c_{p_{2},1}\\
&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
&a_{0,p_{2}-1}&b_{0,p_{2}-1}&a_{1,p_{2}-1}&b_{1,p_{2}-1}&\cdots&a_{p_{2}-1,p_{2}-1}&b_{p_{2}-1,p_{2}-1}&a_{p_{2},p_{2}-1}\\
&c_{0,p_{2}-1}&d_{0,p_{2}-1}&c_{1,p_{2}-1}&d_{1,p_{2}-1}&\cdots&c_{p_{2}-1,p_{2}-1}&d_{p_{2}-1,p_{2}-1}&c_{p_{2},p_{2}-1}\\
&a_{0,p_{2}}&b_{0,p_{2}}&a_{1,p_{2}}&b_{1,p_{2}}&\cdots&a_{p_{2}-1,p_{2}}&b_{p_{2}-1,p_{2}}&a_{p_{2},p_{2}}\\
\text{Row }p-1&c_{0,p_{2}}&d_{0,p_{2}}&c_{1,p_{2}}&d_{1,p_{2}}&\cdots&c_{p_{2}-1,p_{2}}&d_{p_{2}-1,p_{2}}&c_{p_{2},p_{2}}\end{matrix}

Lemma 10.1
.

Let
a_{i,j}
,
b_{i,j}
,
c_{i,j}
and
d_{i,j}
be as above and let
b_{-1,j},b_{p_{2},j},d_{-1,j},d_{p_{2},j}=0
for all
0\leq j\leq p_{2}
. Then,

•

For
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
and
0\leq j\leq p_{2}
,

(10.2)

a_{i,j}=\left(\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}\left(\frac{k}{p_{2}+k-i}\right)^{j-k}\right)\right)^{2},

•

For
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
and
0\leq j\leq p_{2}
,

(10.3)

b_{i,j}=(-1)^{j}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\cdot\frac{i+k+1}{(p_{2}-i+k)^{2(j-k)-1}}\right)\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}k^{2(j-k)},

•

For
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
and
0\leq j\leq p_{2}
,

(10.4)

c_{i,j}={p_{2}+j\choose i+j}\cdot\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}+k-i+1}\right)^{2(j-k)-1},

•

For
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
and
0\leq j\leq p_{2}
,

(10.5)

d_{i,j}=0.

Proof.

These formulae are proven simultaneously by induction on
j
, with the base case (
j=0
) given by

\displaystyle a_{i,0}=1,

\displaystyle b_{i,0}=1,

\displaystyle c_{i,0}={p_{2}\choose i},

\displaystyle d_{i,0}=0.

The above values follow immediately from the definition of row
-1
of a number wall (Definition
1.5
) and the definition of the
p
-Cantor sequence (Definition (
3.2
).

For the induction step, let
0\leq j\leq p_{2}
and assume that

(1)

equations (
10.2
-
10.5
) hold for all
0\leq j^{\prime}<j
,

(2)

for all
0\leq j^{\prime}<j
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}
,
a_{i,j^{\prime}}
and
c_{i,j^{\prime}}
are
nonzero
,

(3)

for all
0\leq j^{\prime}<j
and
0\leq i\leq p_{2}-1
,
b_{i,j^{\prime}}
is
nonzero
.

By assumptions (
2
) and (
3
),
\left(W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{1}^{(p)}\right)[m,n]\right)_{-1\leq m<2j-1,\penalty\ p\leq n<2p}
appears as follows:

Figure 33.
The number wall
W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{1}^{(p)}\right)[m,n]
for
-1\leq m\leq j
and
p\leq n<2p
. The crossed dots indicate that an entry is nonzero.

As seen above,
d_{i,j}
is in the southern outer frame of a window of size one, given by
d_{i,j-1}
. More specifically,
d_{i,j}=H_{1}
(with notation from Figure
1.8
). Furthermore, one has that

\displaystyle E_{1}=d_{i,j-2}=0,

\displaystyle F_{1}=d_{i-1,j-1}=0,

\displaystyle G_{1}=d_{i+1,j-1}=0

and hence, by
FC3
,
d_{i,j}=0
.

Next, assumptions (
2
) and (
3
) also imply that
a_{i,j}
,
b_{i,j}
and
c_{i,j}
are all calculated using
FC1
. Therefore, one obtains the following recurrence relations:

(10.6)

\displaystyle a_{i,j}=\frac{c_{i,j-1}^{2}}{a_{i,j-1}},

\displaystyle b_{i,j}=-\frac{c_{i,j-1}\cdot{c_{i+1,j-1}}}{b_{i,j-1}},

\displaystyle c_{i,j}=\frac{a_{i,j}^{2}-b_{i,j}\cdot b_{i-1,j}}{c_{i,j-1}}\cdotp

These are used to verify equations (
10.2
), (
10.3
) and (
10.4
). As the calculations are elementary, only an outline is provided for each case.

Verifying equation
(
10.2
):

By the induction hypothesis, equations (
10.2
) and (
10.4
), and the first part of equation (
10.6
),

(10.7)

\displaystyle a_{i,j}=\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}\right)\cdot\frac{\prod_{k=0}^{j-2}\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k+1}\right)^{4(j-1-k)-2}}{\prod_{k=1}^{j-2}\left(\frac{k}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2(j-k)-2}}

Separating the
k=j-2
term of the numerator and re-indexing to match the denominator gives

\displaystyle(\ref{eqn: base_canse_ind_a_1})

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{j-1}{p_{2}-i+j-1}\right)^{2}\cdot\prod_{k=0}^{j-2}\left(\frac{k}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2(j-k)}

which is equal to
a_{i,j}
upon combining the terms into one product.

Verifying equation
(
10.3
):

By the induction hypothesis, equations (
10.3
) and (
10.4
), and the second part of equation (
10.6
),

\displaystyle b_{i,j}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j}\cdot{p_{2}+j-1\choose i+j}

\displaystyle\cdot\underbrace{{p_{2}+j-1\choose i+j-1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-2}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{(p_{2}+k-i+1)^{2(j-k)-3}}\cdot\frac{1}{i+k+1}\right)}_{X}

(10.8)

\displaystyle\cdot\underbrace{\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j-2}k^{2(j-k)-2}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-2}(k+1)^{2(j-k)-3}\right)^{2}}_{Y}.

The part of the above equation labelled
X
is dealt with first. Indeed, using the factorial definition of the choose function and factorising out every power of
p_{2}-i
that appears, one has that

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{(p_{2}-i)^{2j-1}}\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-2}\frac{1}{(p_{2}+k-i+1)^{(2j-k)-3}}\right)}_{X_{1}}

\displaystyle\cdot\underbrace{\frac{(p_{2}+j-1)!}{(i+j-1)!\cdot(p_{2}-i-1)!}\cdot\prod_{k=0}^{j-2}\left(\frac{(p_{2}+k)!}{(i+k)!\cdot(p_{2}-i-1)!}\right)^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{i+k+1}}_{X_{2}}.

By re-indexing the product in
X_{1}
, one obtains

(10.9)

X_{1}=\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\frac{1}{(p_{2}-i+k)^{2(j-k)-1}}\cdotp

Next, note that

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j-1\choose i+j}\cdot(i+j)\cdot\prod_{k=0}^{j-2}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\cdot(i+k+1)

Now
Y
is dealt with. Indeed, by re-indexing the second product to begin at
k=1
one obtains that

Y=\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}k^{2(j-k)}.

Inserting
X_{1}
,
X_{2}
and
Y
back into equation (
10.8
) completes the proof in this case.

Verifying equation
(
10.4
):

By splitting the third part of equation (
10.6
) into two summands, applying equations (
10.2
), (
10.3
) and (
10.4
) and simplifying, one obtains

(10.10)

\displaystyle c_{i,j}

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j-1\choose i+j-1}^{3}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-2}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}\left(\frac{k}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2(j-k)+1}\right)

\displaystyle-{p_{2}+j-1\choose i+j-1}\cdot\frac{1}{(p_{2}-i)^{2j-1}}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\cdot\frac{(i+k+1)(i+k)}{(p_{2}-i+k+1)^{2(j-k)-1}}\right)\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}k^{2(j-k)+1}.

Next, factorise to get

(10.11)

\displaystyle(\ref{eqn: base_case_ind_c_1})

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j-1\choose i+j-1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}\left(\frac{k}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2(j-k)+1}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{(p_{2}-i)^{2j-1}\cdot(p_{2}-i+j)}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\cdot(i+k+1)(i+k)\right)\right)

Applying the factorial definition of the choose function, one factorises further:

(10.12)

\displaystyle(\ref{eqn: base_case_ind_c_2})

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j-1\choose i+j-1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}\left(\frac{k}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2(j-k)+1}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\frac{1}{((p_{2}-i)!)^{2j-1}\cdot(p_{2}-i-1)!}\cdot\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\left(\frac{(p_{2}+k)!}{(i+k)!}\right)^{2}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{2}-i}-\frac{i}{(i+j)\cdot(p_{2}-i+j)}\right)

Using that

\frac{1}{p_{2}-i}-\frac{i}{(i+j)\cdot(p_{2}-i+j)}=\frac{j\cdot(p_{2}+j)}{(p_{2}-i)\cdot(i+j)\cdot(p_{2}-i+j)},

one obtains

\displaystyle(\ref{eqn: base_case_ind_c_3})

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j\choose i+j}\cdot\frac{j}{p_{2}-i+j}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}\left(\frac{k}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2(j-k)+1}\right)\cdot\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}.

Re-indexing the first product above to begin at
k=0
establishes equation (
10.4
).

Completing the Base Case

Finally, assumptions
2
and
3
are shown to hold. Indeed,
a_{i,j}
,
b_{i,j}
and
c_{i,j}
are all products of nonzero terms over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and are therefore nonzero themselves.
∎

10.2.
The Inductive Step

Now that the base case has been established, the induction step is proved.

10.2.1.
Preliminaries to the Induction Step

Assume that (
10.1
) holds up to some natural number
h
. The goal is now to show that

\displaystyle\chi\left(\left(W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{h+1}^{(p)}\right)[m,n]\right)_{0\leq m<p^{h},\penalty\ p^{h}\leq n<2p^{h}}\right)=\Pi\left(\Phi_{p}^{h+1}(A)\right)

\displaystyle=\Pi\left(\begin{matrix}\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\cdots&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)\\
\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\cdots&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)\\
\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\cdots&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)\\
\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\cdots&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)\\
\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\cdots&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\cdots&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)\\
\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\cdots&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(F)\\
\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\cdots&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(0)&\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)\\
\end{matrix}\right)

Recall the image of
0,F,E_{N},E_{W},E_{S},E_{E},C_{NE},C_{NW},C_{SW}
and
C_{SE}
under
\Phi_{p}
from Section
5.2
. An easy induction shows that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

Recall
\rho
from equation (
7.13
). Another simple induction shows that

\Pi\left(\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)\right)=\rho\left(\Pi\left(\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)\right)\right)\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ and }\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \Pi\left(\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)\right)=\rho\left(\Pi\left(\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)\right)\right).

Furthermore, define

\widetilde{\textbf{S}}_{h}^{(p)}:=\{0\}_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}\oplus\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}.

Then,

\displaystyle\Pi\left(\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)\right)

\displaystyle=\rho\left(\Pi\left(\Phi_{p}^{h}(A)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\rho\left(\chi\left(\left(W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{h}^{(p)}\right)[m,n]\right)_{0\leq m<p^{h},\penalty\ p^{h}\leq n<2p^{h}}\right)\right)

(10.13)

\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}\text{Corollary \ref{cor: rotate_profile}}\\
=\end{subarray}\chi\left(\left(W_{p}\left(\widetilde{\textbf{S}}_{h}^{(p)}\right)[i,j]\right)_{-1\leq i<p^{h}-1,\penalty\ p^{h}+3\leq j<p^{h}+3}\right)

In other words, the profile of the green square in Figure
26
(with
m=0
and
n=p^{h}+2
) is given by
\Pi\left(\Phi_{p}^{h}(B)\right)
.

10.2.2.
The Strategy for the Induction Step

The plan is to repeatedly apply Construction Lemma
1
followed by Construction Lemma
2
, where the output of Construction Lemma
1
(
2
, respectively) is the input for Construction Lemma
2
(
1
, respectively). To this end, define

r_{S,i,j},\penalty\ a_{S,i,j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ for }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq i<p_{2}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ and }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq j\leq p

and

r_{C,i,j},\penalty\ a_{C,i,j},\penalty\ \text{ and }a_{i,j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ for }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq i\leq p_{2}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ and }\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\leq j\leq p.

These are intended to match the notation from Construction Lemmas
1
and
2
, with the third index denoting how many times either of the Construction Lemmas has been applied before.

Remark 10.2
.

Throughout this proof, it is assumed that
r_{S,i,j},a_{S,i,j},a_{i},r_{C,i,j}
and
a_{C,i,j}
are not equal to zero (with the exception of
a_{S,i,0}
). This is verified in Section
10.3

Applying Construction Lemma 1

To begin, recall that
{p\choose i}=0
for all
i\not\in\mathbb{N}
. Lemma
3.5
states that

10.2.4.
Applying Construction Lemma
1
and
2
Repeatedly

One now applies Construction Lemma
1
and
2
in an alternating fashion, each time using the output of the previous Construction Lemma as the input of the next one. That is, for
0\leq j\leq p_{2}
, each time Construction Lemma
2
is applied, the variables
a_{S,i,2j}
,
r_{S,i,2j}
,
a_{i,2j}
,
a_{C,i,2j}
, and
r_{C,i,2j}
are outputted. These are then are used in Construction Lemma
1
as

\displaystyle m\leftrightarrow 2j\cdot p^{h}

\displaystyle r_{S,i}\leftrightarrow r_{S,i,2j},

\displaystyle a_{S,i}\leftrightarrow a_{S,i,2j},

\displaystyle a_{i}\leftrightarrow a_{i,2j},

\displaystyle r_{C,i}\leftrightarrow r_{C,i,2j},

\displaystyle a_{C,i}\leftrightarrow a_{C,i,2j}.

Similarly, for
0\leq j<p_{2}
, each time Construction Lemma
1
is applied it outputs variables
a_{S,i,2j+1}
,
r_{S,i,2j+1}
,
a_{i,2j+1}
,
a_{C,i,2j+1}
, and
r_{C,i,2j+1}
which are fed into Construction Lemma 2 as inputs

\displaystyle m\leftrightarrow(2j+1)\cdot p^{h}+1

\displaystyle r_{S,i}\leftrightarrow r_{S,i,2j+1},

\displaystyle a_{S,i}\leftrightarrow a_{S,i,2j+1},

\displaystyle a_{i}\leftrightarrow a_{i,2j+1},

\displaystyle r_{C,i}\leftrightarrow r_{C,i,2j+1},

\displaystyle a_{C,i}\leftrightarrow a_{C,i,2j+1}.

Furthermore,

•

each time Construction Lemma
1
is applied, the profile of rows
2j\cdot p^{h}
to
(2j+1)\cdot p^{h}-1
are given by Figure
35
,

•

each time Construction Lemma
2
is applied, the profile of rows
(2j+1)\cdot p^{h}
to
(2j+2)\cdot p^{h}-1
are given by Figure
38
.

The proof concludes when Construction Lemma 1 is applied for the
p_{2}^{\textup{th}}
time. This completes the proof of Theorem
5.2
modulo Remark
10.2
.

10.3.
Verifying Remark
10.2

Finally, it is necessary to verify that every time either Construction Lemma is applied, none of the inputs are zero (with the exception of
a_{S,i,0}
). In this subsection, the values of
a_{S,i,j},r_{S,i,j},a_{i,j},r_{C,i,j}
and
a_{C,i,j}
are computed explicitly.

To begin, note that
a_{i,j}
can be written in terms of
r_{S,i,j}
. Indeed,
a_{i,2j+1}
only appears in the output to Construction Lemma
1
on row
(2j+1)\cdot p^{h}
and similarly
a_{i,2j}
only appears in the output of Construction Lemma
o
n row
2j\cdot p^{h}-1
. These two rows appear as follows:

Figure 39.
Rows
2jp^{h}-1
(
(2j+1)p^{h}
, respectively) are the only rows in which
a_{i,2j}
(
a_{i,2j+1}
, respectively) appear.

Furthermore,
W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{h}(p))[m,p^{h+1}]=1
for all
-1\leq m\leq p^{h+1}
, as it is in the inner frame of the window generated by first
p^{h+1}
zeroes of
W_{p}(\widetilde{\textbf{C}}_{h}(p))
. Therefore, by
FLT
,

(10.16)

a_{i,2j}=\prod_{k=0}^{i-1}r_{S,k,2j}^{2}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ and }\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ a_{i,2j+1}=\prod^{i-1}_{k=0}r_{S,k,2j+1}^{-2}.

Next, Construction Lemma 1 gives recurrence relations for
a_{S,i,2j+1},r_{S,i,2j+1},r_{C,i,2j+1}
and
a_{C,i,2j+1}
in terms of
a_{S,i,2j},r_{S,i,2j},a_{i,2j},r_{C,i,2j}
and
a_{C,i,2j}
. Similarly, Construction Lemma 2 gives recurrence relations for
a_{S,i,2j},r_{S,i,2j},r_{C,i,2j}
and
a_{C,i,2j}
in terms of
a_{S,i,2j+1},r_{S,i,2j+1},a_{i,2j+1},r_{C,i,2j+1}
and
a_{C,i,2j+1}
. Combining these with equation (
10.16
) gives the following 8 recurrence relations:

(10.17)

\displaystyle r_{C,i,2j+1}=r_{C,i,2j}^{-1}

(10.18)

\displaystyle r_{C,i,2j+2}=-\frac{a_{S,i-1,2j+1}}{a_{S,i,2j+1}\cdot r_{C,i,2j+1}\cdot r_{S,i,2j+1}^{2}}

(10.19)

\displaystyle a_{C,i,2j+1}=a_{C,i,2j}

(10.20)

\displaystyle a_{C,i,2j+2}=a_{S,i,2j+1}\cdot r_{S,i,2j+1}^{2}\cdot\left(1+\frac{a_{S,i-1,2j+1}}{r_{S,i,2j+1}^{3}\cdot a_{S,i,2j+1}\cdot r_{C,i,2j+1}}\right)

(10.21)

\displaystyle r_{S,i,2j+1}=-\frac{a_{C,i,2j}\cdot r_{S,i,2j}}{a_{C,i+1,2j}}

(10.22)

\displaystyle r_{S,i,2j+2}=r_{S,i,2j+1}^{-1}

(10.23)

\displaystyle a_{S,i,2j+1}=a_{C,i+1,2j}^{3}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{i}r_{S,k,2j}^{-4}\right)\cdot\left(1+\frac{a_{C,i,2j}\cdot r_{S,i,2j}}{a_{C,i+1,2j}\cdot r_{C,i+1,2j}}\right)

(10.24)

\displaystyle a_{S,i,2j+2}=\frac{r_{S,i,2j+1}^{2}\cdot\prod_{k=0}^{i}r_{S,k,2j+1}^{-4}}{a_{S,i,2j+1}}

with initial values

\displaystyle r_{C,i,0}=1

\displaystyle a_{C,i,0}={p_{2}\choose i}

\displaystyle r_{S,i,0}=1

\displaystyle a_{S,i,0}=0.

It is now established that the following formulae hold:

(10.25)

\displaystyle r_{C,i,2j}=(-1)^{j}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{j}\frac{i+k}{p_{2}-i+k}

(10.26)

\displaystyle r_{C,i,2j+1}=(-1)^{j}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{j}\frac{p_{2}-i+k}{i+k}

(10.27)

\displaystyle a_{C,i,2j+1}=a_{C,i,2j}={p_{2}+j\choose i+j}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}+1-i+k}\right)^{2j-1-2k}\right)

(10.28)

\displaystyle r_{S,i,2j}=(-1)^{j}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\frac{p_{2}-i+k}{i+1+k}\right)

(10.29)

\displaystyle r_{S,i,2j+1}=(-1)^{j+1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}\frac{i+k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)

(10.30)

\displaystyle a_{S,i,2j}=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }j=0\\
{p_{2}+j\choose i+j}^{-1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-2}\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}+k-i+1}\right)^{2j-3-2k}\right)&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

(10.31)

\displaystyle a_{S,i,2j+1}={p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}\cdot\frac{p_{2}+j+1}{j+1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2j+1-2k}\right)

It is easy to verify that each of these values is nonzero for
1\leq j\leq p_{2}
.

10.3.1.
Establishing Equations (
10.25
-
10.31
)

Each of the above formulae is proved by induction, where the bases cases are given by the
j=1
case established in equations (
10.14
) and (
10.15
). In both the inductive step and the base case, the following elementary identity is used frequently: for
x,y\in\mathbb{Z}
with
0\leq y<x

(10.32)

{x\choose y}+{x\choose y+1}={x+1\choose y+1}.

With the exception of identity (
10.32
), there is no special technique required to prove equations (
10.25
-
10.31
). Therefore, an abridged version of the proof is provided for each equation.

Proof of Equation (
10.25
) and hence Equation (
10.26
).

By applying recurrence relation (
10.18
) and the induction hypothesis and then completing some elementary cancellations, one obtains

\displaystyle r_{C,i,2j+2}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j+1}\cdot\frac{{p_{2}+j\choose i+j}}{{p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}}\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}\frac{{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}(p_{2}-i+k)^{2j+1-2k}}{{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}(p_{2}-i+k+1)^{2j+1-2k}}\right)

(10.33)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \cdot\left(\prod^{j}_{k=1}\frac{i+k}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}\frac{p_{2}-i+k}{i+k+1}\right)^{2}

Then, using the definition of the choose function and combining the products yields

(10.34)

\displaystyle(\ref{r_{C,i,2n}_eqn_1})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j+1}\cdot\frac{(i+j+1)}{(p_{2}-i)^{2j+1}}\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j}\left(\frac{(p_{2}-i+k)^{2j+2-2k}(i+k)}{(p_{2}-i+k+1)^{2j+1-2j}}\right)\right)

Expanding out the product and making cancellations gives

\displaystyle(\ref{r_{C,i,2n}_eqn_2})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j+1}\prod_{k=1}^{j+1}\frac{i+k}{p_{2}-i+k},

establishing equation (
10.25
). Equation (
10.26
) follows immediately from recurrence relation (
10.17
).

Proof of Equation (
10.27
).

Using recurrence relation (
10.20
) and combining the products yields

\displaystyle a_{C,i,2j+2}={p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}\cdot\frac{p_{2}+j+1}{n+1}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2j+1-2k}\left(\frac{i+k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2}\right)

(10.35)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(1-\frac{{p_{2}+j\choose i+j}}{{p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}}\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}\frac{{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}}{{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}}\left(\frac{p_{2}-i+k}{p_{2}-i+k+1}\right)^{2j+1-2k}\left(\frac{p_{2}-i+k}{i+k+1}\right)^{3}\right)\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j}\frac{i+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)\right)

Applying the definition of the choose function, factorising out the
k=0
term of the second product and making cancellations gives

\displaystyle(\ref{a_{C,i,2n}_eqn_1})

\displaystyle=\frac{(p_{2}+j)!}{(i+j+1)!(p_{2}-i-1)!}\cdot\frac{p_{2}+j+1}{j+1}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}\left(\frac{(p_{2}+k)!}{(i+k+1)!(p_{2}-i-1)!}\right)^{2}\frac{(k+1)^{2j+1-2k}(i+k+1)^{2}}{(p_{2}-i+k)^{2j+3-2k}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(1-\frac{(i+j+1)(p_{2}-i)}{(i+1)(p_{2}-i+1)^{2j+1}}\prod_{k=1}^{j}\frac{(p_{2}-i+k)^{2j+3-2k}(i+k)}{(p_{2}-i+k+1)^{2j+1-2k}(i+k+1)}\right)

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j+1\choose i+j+1}\frac{(p_{2}-i)^{2j+3}}{j+1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}\frac{(k+1)^{2j+1-2k}}{(p_{2}-i+k)^{2j+3-2k}}\right)

(10.36)

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{(i+j+1)(p_{2}-i)}{(i+1)(p_{2}-i+1)^{2j+1}}\prod_{k=1}^{j}\frac{(p_{2}-i+k)^{2j+3-2k}(i+k)}{(p_{2}-i+k+1)^{2j+1-2k}(i+k+1)}\right).

Next, factorise out the
k=0
term from the denominator of the first product and reindex. Additionally, expand the second product and make cancellations. This yields

\displaystyle(\ref{a_{C,i,2n}_eqn_2})

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j+1\choose i+j+1}\frac{p_{2}-i+j+1}{j+1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k+1}\right)^{2j+1-2k}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{(p_{2}-i)}{(p_{2}-i+j+1)}\right)

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j+1\choose i+j+1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k+1}\right)^{2j+1-2k}\right),

as required.

Proof of equation (
10.28
) and (
10.29
)

By applying the recurrence relation (
10.21
),

(10.37)

\displaystyle r_{S,i,2j+1}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j+1}\cdot\frac{{p_{2}+j\choose i+j}}{{p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}}\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\frac{{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}}{{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}}\frac{p_{2}-i+k}{i+k+1}\left(\frac{p_{2}-i+k}{p_{2}-i+k+1}\right)^{2j-1-2k}.

Applying the definition of the choose function and simplifying gives

(10.38)

\displaystyle(\ref{r_{S,i,2n+1}_eqn_1})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j+1}\frac{i+j+1}{p_{2}-i}\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\frac{(i+k+1)(p_{2}-i+k)}{(p_{2}-i)^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{p_{2}-i+k}{p_{2}-i+k+1}\right)^{2j-1-2k}.

Expanding the above product gives

(\ref{r_{S,i,2n+1}_eqn_2})=(-1)^{j+1}\prod_{k=0}^{j}\frac{i+k+1}{p_{2}-i-k},

as required. Equation (
10.30
) follows from equation (
10.21
) and equation (
10.28
).

Proof of Equation (
10.30
)
.

Using recurrence relation (
10.24
), one obtains

(10.39)

\displaystyle a_{S,i,2j+2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}\frac{i+k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2}\cdot\overbrace{\left(\prod_{h=0}^{i}\prod_{k=0}^{j}\frac{h+k+1}{p_{2}-h+j}\right)^{-4}}^{(\times)}}{{p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}\cdot\frac{p_{2}+j+1}{j+1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2j+1-2k}\right)}

Similarly to the
a_{S,i,2j+1}
calculation, one has that

\displaystyle(\times)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}\frac{(i+k+1)!(p_{2}-i+k-1)!}{(p_{2}+k)!k!}\right)^{-4}

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{-1}\cdot(i+1+k)\cdot\frac{(p_{2}-i+k-1)!}{(p_{2}-i)!k!}\right)^{-4}

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{-1}\cdot(i+1+k)\right)^{-4}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{2}-i}\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}(p_{2}-i+k)^{j-k}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}(k+1)^{-(j-k)}\right)\right)^{-4}

Reinserting this into equation (
10.39
) gives

\displaystyle(\ref{a_{S,i,2n+2}_eqn_1})

\displaystyle=\frac{(p_{2}-i)^{4}\prod_{k=0}^{j}\left(\frac{1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2}\cdot{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{4}(i+k+1)^{-2}}{{p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}\cdot\frac{p_{2}+j+1}{j+1}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2j+1-2k}\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}(p_{2}-i+k)^{4j-4k}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}(k+1)^{-(4j-4k)}\right)}

\displaystyle=Z\cdot W

where

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=\frac{(p_{2}-i)^{4}\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{4}(i+k+1)^{-2}}{{p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}\cdot(p_{2}+j+1)\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=(p_{2}-i)^{-2j+1}{p_{2}+j+1\choose i+j+1}^{-1}\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}

and

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=\frac{(j+1)\prod_{k=0}^{j}\frac{(p_{2}-i+k)^{2j-1-2k}}{(k+1)^{2j+1-k}}}{\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}(p_{2}-i+k)^{4j-4k}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}(k+1)^{-(4j-4k)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}(k+1)^{2j-1-2k}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j}(p_{2}-i+k)^{-(2j-1-2k)}\right)\cdot(p_{2}-i)^{2j-1}.

Reindexing the latter product in
W
to go from
k=0
to
j-1
and multiplying
Z
and
W
together completes the proof.

Proof of Equation (
10.31
)
.

Using recurrence relation (
10.23
), one obtains

\displaystyle a_{S,i,2j+1}

\displaystyle=X\cdot Y,

where

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}^{3}\cdot\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{6}\cdot\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{6j-3-6k}\right)\cdot\underbrace{\left(\prod_{h=0}^{i}\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\frac{p_{2}-h+k}{h+k+1}\right)^{-4}}_{(*)}

(10.40)

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{{p_{2}+j\choose i+j}\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k}^{2}\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k+1}\right)^{2j-1-2k}\right)}{{p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2j-1-2k}\right)}\frac{(-1)^{j}\cdot\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\frac{p_{2}-i+k}{i+k+1}}{(-1)^{j}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{j}\frac{i+k+1}{p_{2}-i+k-1}}\right)

The values of
X
and
Y
are now simplified separately, with the former being done first. Indeed, one swaps the order of the products in
(*)
to get

\displaystyle(*)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\prod_{h=0}^{i}\frac{p_{2}-h+k}{h+k+1}\right)^{-4}

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\frac{(p_{2}+k)!\cdot k!}{(p_{2}-i+k-1)!(i+k+1)!}\right)^{-4}

(10.41)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}\cdot\frac{k!(p_{2}-i-1)!}{(p_{2}-i+k-1)!}\right)^{-4}

Reinserting equation (
10.41
) into
X
gives

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}^{3}\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{6j-3-6k}\right)\left(\underbrace{\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\left(\frac{k!(p_{2}-i-1)!}{(p_{2}-i+k-1)!}\right)^{-4}}_{(**)}\right)

Next, by noting that the first term of
(**)
is equal to
1
, reindexing and combing powers of like terms from the factorials in
(**)
, one arrives at

\displaystyle(**)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=0}^{n-2}\left(\frac{(j+1)!(p_{2}-i-1)!}{(p_{2}-i+j)!}\right)^{-4}

(10.42)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{j=0}^{n-2}\left(\frac{j+1}{p_{2}-i+j}\right)^{n-1-j}\right)^{-4}

Reinserting
(**)
into
X
and using that
6n-3-6j=2n+1-2j
when
j=n-1
, one has

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}^{3}\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2j+1-2k}\right).

Now
Y
is dealt with. The first step towards this is to apply the factorial definition of the choose function and make some cancellations:

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle=1+\left(\frac{i+j+1}{p_{2}-i}\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\left(\frac{i+k+1}{p_{2}-i}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{p_{2}-i+k}{p_{2}-i+k+1}\right)^{2j-2k-1}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\cdot\left.\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}\frac{(p_{2}-i+k)(p_{2}-i+k+1)}{(i+k+1)^{2}}\right)\frac{(p_{2}-i)(p_{2}-i+j-1)}{(i+1)(i+j+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=1+\left(\frac{(p_{2}-i+j-1)(i+1)}{(p_{2}-i)(p_{2}+1-i)^{2j-1}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}\frac{(p_{2}-i+k)^{2j-2k}\cdot(p_{2}-i+k-1)}{(p_{2}+1-i+k)^{2j-1-2k}}\right)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{i+1}{p_{2}-i+j}\cdotp

Finally, combining
X
and
Y
gives

\displaystyle a_{S,i,2j+1}

\displaystyle={p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+j}\right)^{2j+1-2k}\right)\cdot\frac{p_{2}+j+1}{p_{2}-i+j}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{2}+j+1}{j+1}{p_{2}+j\choose i+j+1}\left(\prod_{k=0}^{j}{p_{2}+k\choose i+k+1}^{2}\left(\frac{k+1}{p_{2}-i+k}\right)^{2j+1-2k}\right)

as required.

Appendix A: Variations on a Sequence

Throughout this work, numerous versions of any given sequence are used. The purpose of this appendix is to help the reader keep track of them.

Transformations on Sequences of Arbitrary Length

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\infty
and let
\textbf{S}=(s_{i})_{0\leq i\leq n}
be a sequence over some integral domain
\mathcal{R}
. Then,

•

For
k\in\mathcal{R}
,
k\cdot\mathbf{S}=(k\cdot s_{i})_{0\leq i\leq n}
,

Transformations of finite sequences

Let
\textbf{S}=(s_{i})_{0\leq i\leq l}
be finite sequence of length
l+1\in\mathbb{N}
over some integral domain
\mathcal{R}
. Furthermore, recall that for
\oplus
denotes concatenation of sequences. Then,

•

\widetilde{\textbf{S}}:=\{0\}_{0\leq i\leq l}\oplus\textbf{S}\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i\leq l}
;

•

\overset{\text{\tiny$\bm{\leftrightarrow}$}}{\textbf{{S}}}=(\overset{\text{\tiny$\bm{\leftrightarrow}$}}{s}_{i})_{0\leq i\leq n}
, where
\overset{\text{\tiny$\bm{\leftrightarrow}$}}{s}_{i}=s_{l-i}
.

Transformations of Infinite Sequence

Let
\textbf{R}=(r_{i})_{i\geq 0}
be an infinite sequence over some integral domain
\mathcal{R}
. Then, the following variations on
R
are defined:

•

\textbf{R}^{L}:=(x_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
such that
x_{i}=\begin{cases}r_{i}&\text{ if }i\geq 0\\
0&\text{ otherwise,}\end{cases}

•

\textbf{C}^{(p,L)}=\left(c_{i}^{(p)}\right)_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
where
c^{(p)}_{i}=0
for all
i<0
.

Geometric Sequences

For
h\in\mathbb{N}
and
r,a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, let

•

\textbf{G}_{h,s}(r,a)=\left(a\cdot r^{i}\right)_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}
,

•

\textbf{G}_{h,\ell}(r,a)=\left(a\cdot r^{i}\right)_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}.

Transformation of the
p
-Cantor Sequence

Some variations and transformations only apply to the
p
-Cantor sequence,
\textbf{C}(p)=\left(c^{(p)}_{i}\right)_{i\geq 0}
. Let
r,a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
and let
h\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, let

•

c_{i}^{(r,a)}=a\cdot r^{i}\cdot c_{i}^{(p)}
,

•

\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}{(r,a)}:=\left(ar^{i}c^{(p)}\right)_{0\leq i<p^{h}}

•

\textbf{C}_{h}^{(p)}=\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}{(1,1)}
,

•

\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{h}^{(p)}{(r,a)}:=\{0\}_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}\oplus\{a\cdot r^{i}\cdot c^{(p)}_{i}\}_{0\leq i<p^{h}}\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}-1}
,

•

\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{h}^{(p)}=\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{h}^{(p)}{(1,1)}
.

•

\overline{\textbf{C}}^{(p)}_{h}(r^{\prime},a^{\prime},r^{\prime\prime},a^{\prime\prime}):=\{0\}_{i=1,2}\oplus\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}(r^{\prime},a^{\prime})\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i<p^{h}}\oplus\textbf{C}^{(p)}_{h}(r^{\prime\prime},a^{\prime\prime})\oplus\{0\}_{i=1,2}

Transformation of the

p
-Singer Sequence

Similarly to above, the following notation is specific to the
p
-Singer sequence.

•

s_{i}^{(r,a)}=a\cdot r^{i}\cdot s_{i}^{(p)}
,

•

\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}=(s^{(p)}_{i})_{0\leq i<p^{h}+2}.

•

\textbf{S}^{(p)}_{h}{(r,a)}:=\left(ar^{i}s^{(p)}\right)_{0\leq i<p^{h}+2}

•

\widetilde{\textbf{S}}_{h}^{(p)}:=\{0\}_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}\oplus\textbf{S}_{h}^{(p)}\oplus\{0\}_{0\leq i\leq p^{h}+1}.

Appendix B: Number Wall Pictures

See below for pictures of the number wall of the
p
-Cantor sequence for
p=3
,
5
and
7
. Specifically, each picture is of
W_{p}\left(\textbf{C}^{(p,L)}\right)[m,n]
for
-1\leq m\leq p^{h}
and
0\leq n\leq p^{h}
where
h
is some small natural number. Furthermore, the same colour scheme is used in each instance: zero entries in yellow, nonzero entries in a shade of blue in order from darkest to lightest.

Figure 40.
W_{3}\left(\textbf{C}^{(3,L)}\right)[m,n]
for
-2\leq m\leq 3^{6}
and
0\leq n\leq 3^{6}

Figure 41.
W_{5}\left(\textbf{C}^{(5,L)}\right)[m,n]
for
-2\leq m\leq 5^{4}
and
0\leq n\leq 5^{4}

Figure 42.
W_{7}\left(\textbf{C}^{(7,L)}\right)[m,n]
for
-2\leq m\leq 7^{3}
and
0\leq n\leq 7^{3}
